Matching Engine

NadaTrade runs one independent matching engine per instrument, each owning a central limit order book (CLOB). The engine processes OrderCommand variants — New, Cancel, Replace, ClockTick, SessionTransition, Scenario, and Status — and emits ExchangeEvent variants back to the system.

1. Architecture overview

Each MatchingEngine is keyed by InstrumentId (the instrument name string). The OrderRouter (crates/nadatrade-matching/src/router.rs) dispatches incoming commands to the correct engine via a HashMap<InstrumentId, MatchingEngine>.

The CLOB itself (Clob struct in clob.rs:17–25) uses deterministic data structures throughout:

#![allow(unused)]
fn main() {
pub struct Clob {
    pub bids: BTreeMap<Price, VecDeque<RestingOrder>>,
    pub asks: BTreeMap<Price, VecDeque<RestingOrder>>,
    by_order_id: HashMap<OrderId, (Price, Side)>,
    pub by_client_id: HashMap<(ParticipantId, String), OrderId>,
    pub last_trade_price: Option<Price>,
    pub stop_book: StopBook,
    pub expiries: BTreeMap<DateTime<Utc>, Vec<OrderId>>,
}
}
  • BTreeMap<Price, ...> — price levels are ordered. Bids iterate descending (best = highest, via .last_key_value()). Asks iterate ascending (best = lowest, via .first_key_value()). This guarantees deterministic price-level iteration.
  • VecDeque<RestingOrder> — within each price level, orders are FIFO (price-time priority). New orders .push_back(), fills .pop_front().
  • by_order_id — O(1) lookup for cancels and replaces.
  • by_client_id — O(1) duplicate ClOrdID detection, keyed on (participant, client_order_id).
  • No HashMap iteration in hot paths — matching walks BTreeMap entries only.

2. Order types

NadaTrade supports six order types (OrderType enum, enums.rs:13–20):

OrderTypeDescription
MarketFills immediately at the best available opposite price. Must not specify price, stop_price, or display_qty. Requires Ioc, Fok, or Day TIF. If the opposite book is empty → OrderRejected(MarketOrderNoLiquidity).
LimitRests at a specified price (must be present and a multiple of tick_size). Supports all 7 TIF variants.
StopParks in the Stop Book with a trigger_price. When the market crosses the trigger (bid rises above for buys, ask falls below for sells), the order triggers and becomes a Market order (engine.rs:457). Requires stop_price; must not have price. TIF: Day, Gtc, Gtd.
StopLimitLike Stop but triggers into a Limit order at the specified price. Requires both price and stop_price. TIF: Day, Gtc, Gtd.
MarketToLimitLike Market but after filling what it can, the unfilled remainder rests as a Limit at the last trade price (engine.rs:836). Must not specify price or stop_price. TIF: Day, Ioc.
IcebergDisplays only display_qty on the order book; the rest (qty − display_qty) is in a hidden reserve. When the visible portion is exhausted, it replenishes from the hidden reserve and re-enters at the back of the price queue (loses time priority). Requires price and a valid display_qty (must be > 0 and ≤ qty). TIF: Day, Gtc, Gtd.

Market/MarketToLimit vs empty book

Both Market and MarketToLimit are rejected immediately if the opposite side has no resting orders (clob.rs:132–143).

3. Time-in-Force (TIF) matrix

The function validate_tif() in tif_matrix.rs enforces a 6 × 7 = 42-cell compatibility table. Incompatible combinations produce RejectReason::IncompatibleOrderTypeAndTif.

TIF ↓ / OrderType →LimitMarketStopStopLimitMarketToLimitIceberg
Day✓✓✓✓✓✓
Gtc✓✗✓✓✗✓
Gtd✓✗✓✓✗✓
Ioc✓✓✗✗✓✗
Fok✓✓✗✗✗✗
AtOpen✓✗✗✗✗✗
AtClose✓✗✗✗✗✗

TIF behaviours

TIFMeaning
DayGood until end of the trading session. On session close (SessionState::Closed), all Day orders on the CLOB and Stop Book are cancelled with CancelReason::SessionEnd (engine.rs:130–134).
GtcGood-Till-Cancelled. Survives session boundaries. Can be purged at EOD per-participant via eod_purge_participants (emits OrderExpired(DayEndGtc)). Also can be bulk-expired via ScenarioEvent::ExpireAllGtc.
GtdGood-Till-Date. Carries a DateTime<Utc> expiry. Checked every ClockTick against sim_time: if expiry ≤ sim_time, the order is removed and OrderExpired(GtdExpired) is emitted (clob.rs:448–469). GTD stop orders in the Stop Book are also checked (engine.rs:142–174).
IocImmediate-or-Cancel. Fills whatever is possible against the current book, then cancels the remainder. See IOC below.
FokFill-or-Kill. The entire quantity must fill or nothing at all. See FOK below.
AtOpenValid only during PreOpen and OpeningAuction states. Unfilled portions expire with OrderExpired(AuctionUnfilled) after the opening auction uncross (auction.rs:211–230).
AtCloseValid only during ClosingAuction. Same unfilled-expiry semantics as AtOpen.

Field validation per order type

validate_tif() also checks required/optional fields (tif_matrix.rs:49–96):

  • Limit: requires price; rejects stop_price and display_qty.
  • Market: rejects all of price, stop_price, and display_qty.
  • Stop: requires stop_price; rejects price.
  • StopLimit: requires both price and stop_price.
  • MarketToLimit: rejects all of price, stop_price, and display_qty.
  • Iceberg: requires price and display_qty where display_qty > 0 and display_qty ≤ qty.

4. Order lifecycle

All orders follow this state machine:

   ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │                                                      │
   ▼                                                      │
  New ──► Accepted ──► (Partial Fill ──► Accepted ──► ...) ──► Filled
   │                       │                                   ▲
   │                       │                                   │
   └──► Rejected           └──► Cancelled                      │
   └──► Expired            └──► Expired                        │
  • New → Accepted: Order passes session gating, TIF validation, lot size, min/max qty, price tick size, and price band checks. OrderAccepted event emitted.
  • New → Rejected: Fails any defence-in-depth check. OrderRejected event emitted with a specific RejectReason.
  • New → Expired: Market order hits empty book, FOK unfeasible. OrderExpired emitted.
  • Accepted → Partial Fill: On each trade, OrderPartiallyFilled events are emitted for both the resting order (aggressor=false) and the incoming order (aggressor=true).
  • Accepted → Filled: When remaining_qty reaches zero. OrderFilled emitted.
  • Accepted → Cancelled: Client cancel request, STP, session end, scenario, or instrument delisting.
  • Accepted → Expired: IOC unfilled remainder, FOK not feasible, GTD time reached, AtOpen/AtClose unfilled in auction, Day order at EOD with GTC EOD purge.

5. Price-time priority

The CLOB enforces strict price-time priority (clob.rs):

  1. Best price first: Bids are matched from highest to lowest price. Asks are matched from lowest to highest.
  2. FIFO within a price level: Orders at the same price are matched in insertion order via VecDeque. pop_front() takes the oldest order.
  3. New resting orders enter at the back: push_back() — they yield to all existing orders at that price.
  4. Partial fills keep position: A partially filled order (with remaining display_qty > 0) is pushed back to the front of the queue via push_front() (clob.rs:371), preserving its time priority over orders that came later.
  5. Iceberg replenishment loses priority: When an iceberg’s visible portion is exhausted and it replenishes from hidden reserves, it is pushed to the back (clob.rs:369), yielding to all other orders at that level.

6. Self-Trade Prevention (STP)

STP prevents a participant from trading against their own resting orders. The StpMode enum (enums.rs:48–55) has four variants, configured per-order via the stp_mode field:

CancelAggressive (StpMode::CancelAggressive)

The resting order is put back at the front of its price queue (retains time priority via push_front). The aggressive (incoming) order is cancelled. If no prior fills occurred in this matching cycle, an OrderRejected(StpCancelled) is emitted. If there were prior fills, an OrderCancelled(StpCancelAggressive) is emitted (clob.rs:205–228).

CancelResting (StpMode::CancelResting)

The resting order is removed from the book and cancelled with OrderCancelled(StpCancelResting). The aggressive order continues matching against other resting orders (clob.rs:229–244).

CancelBoth (StpMode::CancelBoth)

Both orders are cancelled. The resting order emits OrderCancelled(StpCancelBoth). The incoming order emits OrderCancelled(StpCancelBoth) if there were prior fills, or OrderRejected(StpCancelled) if no fills occurred (clob.rs:245–288).

None (StpMode::None)

No STP check. Orders from the same participant can match — this is allowed for market makers who run separate trading bots under one participant ID.

7. Stop orders

Stop orders rest in the StopBook (stop_book.rs:12–15), not in the CLOB:

#![allow(unused)]
fn main() {
pub struct StopBook {
    pub buy_stops: BTreeMap<Price, VecDeque<StopOrder>>,
    pub sell_stops: BTreeMap<Price, VecDeque<StopOrder>>,
    pub by_order_id: HashMap<OrderId, (Price, bool)>,
}
}

Each StopOrder bundles a RestingOrder with its trigger_price.

Trigger conditions

Scanned after every trade via stop_book.scan(last_trade_price) (stop_book.rs:60–87):

SideTriggers when
Buy stoplast_trade_price >= trigger_price (market rises to or above the stop)
Sell stoplast_trade_price <= trigger_price (market falls to or below the stop)

Scan uses BTreeMap range queries: buy_stops.range(..=last_trade) returns all buy stops with trigger ≤ last trade, ascending by trigger price. sell_stops.range(last_trade..) returns all sell stops with trigger ≥ last trade, descending by trigger price.

Triggered behaviour

When triggered (engine.rs:456–460):

  • Stop → converts to OrderType::Market (no limit price).
  • StopLimit → converts to OrderType::Limit with the order’s price field as the limit.

The triggered order is then passed to clob.match_order() with the converted type.

Stop cascade

Stop execution uses a breadth-first cascade queue (engine.rs:446–482):

  1. Initial scan of the Stop Book against last_trade_price.
  2. All triggered orders are added to a VecDeque.
  3. Each order is popped, matched, and emitted. Each match may update last_trade_price.
  4. After each individual stop-order match, a fresh scan of the Stop Book is run. Newly triggered orders are appended to the back of the queue.
  5. This repeats until the queue is empty — stops can cascade: one stop’s fill triggers another stop, and so on.

After the stop cascade, the circuit breaker check runs (see Circuit breaker).

GTD stop expiry

GTD stop orders are expired on ClockTick by scanning both buy_stops and sell_stops for orders whose Gtd(expiry) time has been reached or passed (engine.rs:142–174).

Day stop EOD cleanup

On session close, cancel_all_stop_day_orders() (clob.rs:540–558) partitions each stop-book price level: Day orders are removed, non-Day orders are retained. The removed Day orders emit OrderCancelled(SessionEnd).

8. Iceberg orders

Iceberg orders display only a fraction of their total quantity on the order book. The RestingOrder struct tracks this through four fields (resting_order.rs:14–18):

FieldMeaning
original_qtyTotal quantity at order entry. Used in replace logic.
remaining_qtyTotal unfilled quantity (visible + hidden).
display_qtyCurrently visible quantity on the book.
hidden_qtyRemaining hidden reserve.
original_displayThe initial display_qty cap; used to bound replenishments.

During matching (clob.rs:294)

A fill consumes fill_qty = min(incoming_remaining, resting.display_qty). Both remaining_qty and display_qty are reduced by fill_qty. Only the visible portion is matched against — the hidden reserve is invisible to aggressors.

Replenishment (resting_order.rs:39–49)

When display_qty drops to zero but hidden_qty > 0:

  1. iceberg_replenish() refills display_qty to min(original_display, hidden_qty).
  2. The replenished order is push_back() into its price level — loses time priority (clob.rs:369).
  3. Returns the replenished amount so the caller can track the new visible size.

Non-replenished (partial fill, display still > 0)

If display_qty is still positive after the fill (partial fill of the visible portion only), the order is push_front() — retains time priority (clob.rs:371).

FOK and iceberg visibility

The FOK feasibility check (clob.rs:414–443) counts remaining_qty (total, including hidden), not just display_qty. A FOK order can succeed against an iceberg even if the visible portion alone would be insufficient.

9. FOK (Fill-or-Kill)

FOK orders must fill in their entirety against the current order book, or fail entirely with no partial fills and no resting remainder.

Feasibility pre-check (clob.rs:414–443)

Before matching begins, fok_feasible() walks the opposite book at all acceptable prices (respecting limit price constraints) and sums remaining_qty across all resting orders. It counts the full quantity, including iceberg hidden reserves.

If the total available quantity at acceptable prices is less than the FOK order’s quantity → OrderExpired(FokNotFilled) is emitted immediately, with no TradePrinted events generated at all.

If feasible, the FOK proceeds through the normal matching loop. Since the feasibility check guarantees sufficient quantity exists, the FOK is guaranteed to fully fill — no remainder handling is needed.

10. IOC (Immediate-or-Cancel)

IOC orders fill whatever is possible against the current book, then cancel the remainder.

Matching proceeds normally through the loop in clob.match_order(). After all possible fills, if remaining_qty > 0:

  • If the order is Ioc or Fok, or the order type is Market → the unfilled remainder emits OrderExpired(IocNotFilled) (engine.rs:822–832).
  • This also applies to Market + Day orders: after matching, any unfilled portion is treated as IOC-like and expired.
  • MarketToLimit + Day orders: after matching, the unfilled portion rests as a Limit order at the last trade price (or zero if no trade occurred) rather than expiring.

11. Replace semantics

Order replacement (engine.rs:945–1178) first removes the existing order from the CLOB by its (participant, orig_client_id) key, then processes a new order.

Validation checks

Before replacement proceeds:

  • Session must accept cancels (Trading, PreOpen, auction states — not Closed or TradingHalt).
  • Target order must exist (by orig_client_id) → else CancelRejected(UnknownOrder).
  • new_client_id must not collide with an existing order from the same participant → else CancelRejected(DuplicateClOrdId).
  • new_qty must not be less than the quantity already filled → else CancelRejected(InvalidReplaceParams).

Decrease-only: keeps priority (engine.rs:1019–1049)

When both conditions are true:

  • new_qty ≤ original_qty (quantity not increased)
  • new_price == existing.price (price unchanged)

The order is modified in-place:

original_qty = new_qty
remaining_qty = new_qty - filled_qty

The order is reinserted into the book via clob.insert() which does push_back(), but since this is a decrease only (qty reduced, price same), it retains its position. No new matching is performed — the order just updates its size. An OrderReplaced event is emitted.

Price change or quantity increase: loses priority (engine.rs:1052–1178)

When either condition is true:

  • new_qty > original_qty (quantity increased)
  • new_price != existing.price (any price change, better or worse)

The old order is removed, an OrderReplaced event is emitted, then the order re-enters as a new order:

  1. match_order() is called with the new quantity and price.
  2. The replacement order gets a new OrderId (fields.id).
  3. Any fills against the current book happen immediately.
  4. If unfilled, the remainder rests as a new order at the back of the queue (clob.insert → push_back).
  5. After matching, the stop cascade and circuit breaker checks run on the new trades.

12. Circuit breaker

The circuit breaker (engine.rs:484–506) triggers when the last trade price deviates too far from the reference price.

Trigger condition

|last_trade − ref_price| / ref_price > price_band

Where:

  • ref_price is the instrument’s reference_price (set via config, auction, or scenario).
  • price_band is the instrument’s price_band (e.g. 0.10 = 10%).

If ref_price == 0, price_band == 0, or last_trade_price is None, the check is skipped.

On trigger

  • The session is forced into SessionState::TradingHalt { reason: CircuitBreaker, until: sim_time + halt_duration_secs }.
  • In this state, all new orders are rejected with RejectReason::InstrumentHalted and all cancels/replaces are rejected too.
  • After halt_duration_secs elapses on a ClockTick, the session transitions to ReopeningAuction, then to Trading.

When checked

The circuit breaker check runs as part of run_stop_cascade_and_circuit_breaker(), which is called:

  • After every process_new match cycle.
  • After every process_replace match cycle.

The stop cascade runs first (so all stop triggers from a trade are processed), then the CB check evaluates the final last_trade_price.

13. Auction

Auction scheduled states

The session state machine drives auctions. On transition from an auction to Trading (or Closed), run_auction_uncross() fires:

TransitionAuction type
OpeningAuction → TradingAuctionType::Opening
ReopeningAuction → TradingAuctionType::Reopening
ClosingAuction → ClosedAuctionType::Closing

Call market algorithm (auction.rs:27–253)

The uncross algorithm proceeds in three tie-break rounds:

Round 0 — Find candidates: For every price level present in either side of the book, compute:

  • buy_demand = sum of all bid quantities at prices ≥ candidate.
  • sell_supply = sum of all ask quantities at prices ≤ candidate.
  • volume = min(buy_demand, sell_supply).
  • surplus = buy_demand − sell_supply.

Tie-break 1 — Maximize volume: Retain only the candidates with the largest volume.

Tie-break 2 — Minimize absolute surplus + market pressure: Retain candidates with the smallest |surplus|.

  • If all remaining candidates have surplus > 0 (buy pressure) → keep only the highest price.
  • If all remaining candidates have surplus < 0 (sell pressure) → keep only the lowest price.

Tie-break 3 — Reference price proximity: Sort remaining candidates by proximity to reference_price. If tied, prefer the higher price.

The first candidate is the clearing price.

Trade allocation (auction.rs:292–358)

Orders are collected in priority order:

  • Bids: descending price (highest first). Within a price level, FIFO.
  • Asks: ascending price (lowest first). Within a price level, FIFO.

A simplified two-phase allocation fills orders in priority order up to the traded_qty cap. Buy fills and sell fills are paired to produce TradePrinted events.

Post-uncross

  • A ReferencePriceChanged event is emitted (source: Auction), updating the reference price to the clearing price.
  • An AuctionUncrossed event is emitted with the clearing price, traded quantity, imbalance quantity, and imbalance side.
  • AtOpen/AtClose orders that were not fully filled emit OrderExpired(AuctionUnfilled).
  • The CLOB is updated: filled orders are reduced or removed, unfilled auction-TIF orders are removed.
  • After applying auction events, the stop cascade and circuit breaker are run.

14. End-of-Day cleanup

When the session transitions to SessionState::Closed (either via ClockTick or SessionTransition), the engine executes three cleanup operations (engine.rs:129–136):

1. Cancel all Day orders on the CLOB

clob.cancel_all_day_orders() (clob.rs:508–537) scans both bids and asks, collects all orders with tif == Day, removes them via remove_by_id(), and emits OrderCancelled(SessionEnd) for each.

2. Cancel all Day orders in the Stop Book

clob.cancel_all_stop_day_orders() (clob.rs:540–558) partitions each stop-book price level, removing Day orders while retaining non-Day orders. Emits OrderCancelled(SessionEnd).

3. Expire GTC orders (per-participant EOD purge)

expire_eod_gtc() (engine.rs:382–420) scans both books for GTC orders belonging to participants listed in eod_purge_participants. Each matching order emits OrderExpired(DayEndGtc). If eod_purge_participants is empty, this step is skipped.

Additionally, GTD order expiry runs on every ClockTick regardless of session state (engine.rs:139).

15. Instruments

TOML definition

Instruments are defined in TOML files under the instruments/ directory and hot-reloaded via the notify file watcher in nadatrade-refdata. The InstrumentConfig struct (instrument.rs:10–39) maps directly to TOML keys.

Hot reload

The InstrumentRegistry (in nadatrade-refdata) watches the instruments/ directory for changes. When a TOML file is added, modified, or removed, a RegistryChange event (Added, Updated, or Delisted) is broadcast to all subscribers.

16. Defence-in-depth validation summary

Every new order passes through these checks in engine.process_new() before matching (engine.rs:566–898):

#CheckReject reason
1Session state gating — which order types and TIFs are allowed in the current session stateSessionNotAcceptingOrders, InstrumentHalted
2TIF compatibility (validate_tif()) — 42-cell matrix + field validationIncompatibleOrderTypeAndTif, InvalidPrice, InvalidQuantity
3Lot size — qty % lot_size == 0InvalidQuantity
4Min/max qty — min_qty ≤ qty ≤ max_qtyInvalidQuantity
5Price tick size — price % tick_size == 0 (for limit/stop-limit/iceberg)InvalidPrice
6Price band — `price − ref_price
7Market order against empty bookMarketOrderNoLiquidity
8FOK feasibility pre-checkFokNotFilled (OrderExpired)
9STP — self-trade detection during matchingStpCancelled, StpCancelAggressive, StpCancelResting, StpCancelBoth

See Also

  • Sessions & Auctions — how session states gate order entry and drive auctions
  • Risk Pipeline — the 11-point pretrade check that runs before orders reach the engine
  • Simulation — how ClockTick drives session transitions and stop expiry
  • Glossary & Cheat Sheet — order type and TIF quick reference

Sessions

Each NadaTrade instrument runs an independent session state machine modelled after real exchange phases: pre-open, auctions, continuous trading, halts, and close. The machine is driven exclusively by SimClock ticks and scenario/circuit-breaker events, keeping all book mutations single-threaded on the matching actor.

Session Types

Every instrument declares one of two session types in its TOML config:

Session typeDescription
continuous_24x7Always Trading; never opens, closes, or auctions. Only TradingHalt (scenario/circuit-breaker) can interrupt it.
session_basedFollows a daily schedule: PreOpen → OpeningAuction → Trading → ClosingAuction → Closed.

State Machine — 7 States

StateDescription
ClosedOutside trading hours. No new orders accepted; cancels/replaces rejected. Books are empty if end-of-day cleanup ran.
PreOpenOrder-entry only. Limit, Stop, StopLimit, and Iceberg orders (with Day, GTC, GTD, or AtOpen TIF) are collected into the book. No matching occurs. Market, IOC, and FOK orders are rejected.
OpeningAuctionAuction collection phase. Only Limit orders (Day, GTC, GTD, AtOpen) are accepted. At the end of the auction, uncross() determines the opening price using the call-market algorithm and generates fill events.
TradingContinuous matching. All valid order-type × TIF combinations are accepted. Matching, stop triggers, and circuit-breaker checks run on every new order.
TradingHalt { reason, until }Trading is suspended. The reason is one of CircuitBreaker, ManualHalt, or TechnicalHalt; until is the UTC timestamp when the halt may expire (if a duration was set). New orders are rejected; cancels and replaces are still accepted.
ReopeningAuctionOccurs after a TradingHalt expires with auto_reopen = true. Only Limit orders (Day, GTC, GTD) are accepted. The uncross price becomes the new reference price, re-arming the price band.
ClosingAuctionEnd-of-day auction collection. Only Limit orders (Day, GTC, GTD, AtClose) are accepted. After uncross, the state transitions to Closed and end-of-day cleanup runs.

Note: A Suspended state is not currently implemented. Instruments that are delisted transition directly to Closed.

State Transition Diagram

 Closed ──(pre_open_start)──► PreOpen ──(opening_auction_start)──► OpeningAuction
                                                                       │
                                (opening_auction_uncross → UNCROSS)     │
                                                                       ▼
        ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────► Trading ◄──────────────┐
        │                                                             │                 │
        │                     circuit breaker / scenario halt          │    manual       │
        │                                                             ▼    resume       │
        │                                                     TradingHalt               │
        │                          (halt_duration elapsed          │                    │
        │                           & auto_reopen = true)          ▼                    │
        │                                                   ReopeningAuction            │
        │                         (reopening_duration → UNCROSS)  │                     │
        └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘                     │
                                                                                       │
 Trading ──(closing_auction_start)──► ClosingAuction ──(uncross)──► Closed             │
                                                                                       │
                                                          (next day)                  ┘

Schedule-Driven Transitions

SessionSchedule::compute() builds a per-day schedule from the instrument’s SessionConfig, using timezone-aware NaiveTime fields and a trading_days allow-list.

Typical trading day (Sao Paulo time)

TimeState
09:45PreOpen begins
09:55OpeningAuction begins (continuous_start minus auction_duration)
10:00Auction uncross → Trading begins
17:30ClosingAuction begins
17:40Closing uncross → Closed

On each ClockTick, SessionStateMachine::on_tick() checks whether sim_time has crossed the next scheduled boundary. If it has, the machine transitions to the next state and the matching engine performs any associated work (auction uncross, EOD cleanup).

Timings for the schedule fields:

Config fieldDefaultDescription
pre_open_start—NaiveTime when PreOpen begins.
continuous_start—NaiveTime when continuous trading begins.
opening_auction_duration_secs300Seconds before continuous_start when the opening auction phase starts.
closing_auction_start—NaiveTime when the closing auction begins.
closing_auction_duration_secs600Duration of the closing auction in seconds.
reopening_auction_duration_secs300Duration of the reopening auction after a halt expires.
timezoneAmerica/Sao_PauloIANA timezone for interpreting NaiveTime fields.

Example config

[instruments.PETR4.session]
type = "session_based"
pre_open_start = "09:45:00"
continuous_start = "10:00:00"
opening_auction_duration_secs = 300
closing_auction_start = "17:30:00"
closing_auction_duration_secs = 600
timezone = "America/Sao_Paulo"

Event-Driven Transitions

Circuit Breaker

The circuit breaker fires after any trade print in Trading when |last_trade − reference_price| / reference_price > price_band. The flow depends on configuration:

  • auto_reopen = true with halt_duration_secs set: Trading → TradingHalt { reason: CircuitBreaker, until } → (halt expires) → ReopeningAuction → (auction uncross) → Trading. The uncross price becomes the new reference price.

  • auto_reopen = false or no duration: Trading → TradingHalt { reason: CircuitBreaker, until: None }. The halt persists until a manual resume via ScenarioEvent::ResumeInstrument.

Orders remain in the book during a halt; cancels and replaces are allowed.

Manual Halt

A ScenarioEvent::HaltInstrument or an admin command triggers:

Trading → TradingHalt { reason: ManualHalt, until }

Manual Resume

Per design decision, ScenarioEvent::ResumeInstrument (or an admin command) transitions directly from TradingHalt to Trading without a reopening auction. This lets operators resume trading immediately after investigating a halt.

Order Gating

Which order types are accepted depends on the current session state:

StateNew ordersCancelsReplacesMatching
ClosedNone (SessionNotAcceptingOrders)None (SessionNotAcceptingCancels)None—
PreOpenLimit, Stop, StopLimit, Iceberg (Day, GTC, GTD, AtOpen)YesYes (no re-match)None — collect only
OpeningAuctionLimit (Day, GTC, GTD, AtOpen)YesYes (no re-match)None — collect; uncross at end
TradingAll valid type × TIF combosYesYesContinuous
TradingHaltNone (InstrumentHalted)YesYes (no re-match)None
ReopeningAuctionLimit (Day, GTC, GTD)YesYes (no re-match)None — collect; uncross at end
ClosingAuctionLimit (Day, GTC, GTD, AtClose)YesYes (no re-match)None — collect; uncross at end
  • IOC, FOK, Market orders are only accepted in Trading.
  • AtOpen orders are accepted in PreOpen and OpeningAuction; AtClose orders in ClosingAuction.
  • Stops submitted during PreOpen rest in the Stop Book and are first evaluated against the opening auction print.

Trading Calendar

The trading_days list in SessionConfig controls which days the schedule applies:

trading_days = ["Mon", "Tue", "Wed", "Thu", "Fri"]
  • "Mon-Fri" (weekdays only) — typical for equity exchanges.
  • "Mon-Sun" — all days, including weekends.
  • An empty list or ["Mon", "Tue", "Wed", "Thu", "Fri", "Sat", "Sun"] — every day is a trading day.
  • On non-trading days, SessionSchedule::compute() returns all None values and the state remains Closed.

A holiday calendar is not currently implemented (future work).

End-of-Day Cleanup

When the state transitions to Closed (after the closing uncross), the following runs in order:

  1. AtClose remainders — unfilled AtClose orders are expired with ExpireReason::AuctionUnfilled (by the uncross step itself).
  2. Day orders — all Day orders in the CLOB and Stop Book are cancelled with CancelReason::SessionEnd.
  3. GTD expiry — GTD orders whose expiry_date ≤ today are expired with ExpireReason::GtdExpired. Stop Book GTD stops are also purged.
  4. GTC EOD purge — GTC orders from participants with gtc_eod_purge = true in their ParticipantConfig are expired with ExpireReason::DayEndGtc.
  5. Remaining GTC/GTD orders persist in the book across Closed into the next PreOpen.

On OpeningAuction → Trading: AtOpen remainders are expired (AuctionUnfilled).

GTD and Day expiries are driven by SimClock ticks — the matching actor checks every sim-time second whether any GTD timestamps have elapsed.

Circuit Breaker Configuration

[instruments.PETR4.circuit_breaker]
enabled = true
halt_duration_secs = 300
auto_reopen = true
FieldDefaultDescription
enabledfalseWhether the circuit breaker is active for this instrument.
halt_duration_secsNoneHow long the halt lasts before automatic transition.
auto_reopenfalseIf true, transitions to ReopeningAuction after the halt expires. If false, the halt persists until a manual resume.

The trigger threshold is the instrument’s price_band — the maximum allowed deviation from reference_price as a decimal fraction. When breached, the circuit breaker fires and a SessionStateChanged event is emitted with the halt reason and until timestamp.

GTD/Day Expiry

GTD and Day expiry is SimClock-tick-driven (not tokio timers), so it behaves correctly under acceleration and replay. On every ClockTick, the matching actor checks:

  • GTD orders — if sim_time >= expiry_timestamp, the order expires (ExpireReason::GtdExpired).
  • Untriggered GTD stops — stops with GTD TIF that never triggered are expired (ExpireReason::StopNeverTriggered).

Expiry resolution is 1 sim-second.

Session State Change Events

Every transition emits ExchangeEvent::SessionStateChanged:

{
  "type": "session_state_changed",
  "instrument": "PETR4",
  "from": "trading",
  "to": { "trading_halt": { "reason": "circuit_breaker", "until": 1718236800000 } },
  "sim_time": 1718236500000
}

These events are broadcast to all gateways and the event store, allowing downstream consumers (TUI, persistence, metrics) to track state without polling.

See Also

  • Matching Engine — how order types and TIFs interact with session states
  • Simulation — how ClockTick drives session transitions
  • Risk Pipeline — session gating in pretrade checks

Risk Pipeline

The risk pipeline validates every incoming order before it reaches the matching engine, enforcing quantity, price, session, instrument, participant, and position limits. Rejected orders never touch the order book.

Pipeline topology

Orders flow through a linear channel chain:

Gateway (FIX/WS) → LatencySim → PretradeActor → MatchingEngine
  1. Gateway — FIX acceptor or WebSocket server parses the incoming message into an OrderCommand.
  2. LatencySim — an optional middleware task injects per-participant latency into the command channel using the participant’s configured LatencyProfile.
  3. PretradeActor — a standalone async task (crates/nadatrade-pretrade/src/actor.rs:41-133) that receives commands on an mpsc::Receiver, runs the full 11-point risk check, and either forwards valid commands to the engine channel or emits OrderRejected events directly to the broadcast channel.
  4. MatchingEngine — receives only pre-validated commands on its own mpsc::Receiver. The engine also runs a defense-in-depth layer of basic validation as a safety net.

The channel wiring is set up in crates/nadatrade-bin/src/main.rs:237-269:

gateway → latency_cmd_rx → (latency sim) → pretrade_cmd_tx → pretrade_cmd_rx → PretradeActor → engine_cmd_tx → engine_cmd_rx → MatchingEngine

The PretradeActor spawns a background task that subscribes to the event_tx broadcast channel to track active order IDs, last trade prices, and update position ledgers. When a New order arrives, it crosses these live values against the configured risk limits.

11 risk checks

Each check runs in order. On failure, the OrderRejected event is emitted immediately with the corresponding RejectReason. Subsequent checks are skipped.

Check 1: Quantity > 0

Order quantity must be strictly positive (> Decimal::ZERO).

Fails with: InvalidQuantity

Check 2: Price > 0 (by order type)

Validates pricing fields match the order type semantics:

Order typeRequires priceRequires stop_price
Marketnono
MarketToLimitnono
Limityes—
Icebergyes—
Stop—yes
StopLimityesyes

Market orders and Market-to-Limit orders must not supply a price. Limit and Iceberg orders must supply a price. Stop orders require a stop_price. Stop-Limit orders require both.

Fails with: InvalidPrice

Check 3: Session state

Orders are gated by the current session state of the instrument:

  • Closed — rejects all orders with SessionNotAcceptingOrders.
  • TradingHalt — rejects all orders with InstrumentHalted.
  • All other states (PreOpen, OpeningAuction, Trading, ReopeningAuction, ClosingAuction) — pass this check. Finer-grained order-type/TIF gating is done by the engine in its defense-in-depth layer.

Fails with: SessionNotAcceptingOrders or InstrumentHalted

Check 4: Instrument min/max quantity

If the instrument config is available (it is in the PretradeActor), the order quantity must be within [min_qty, max_qty] (inclusive).

Fails with: InvalidQuantity

Check 5: Lot size

If the instrument has a non-zero lot_size, the order quantity must be an integer multiple of it (qty % lot_size == 0).

Fails with: InvalidQuantity

Check 6: Tick size

If the instrument has a non-zero tick_size and the order supplies a price, the price must be an integer multiple of tick size (price % tick_size == 0).

Fails with: InvalidPrice

Check 7: Price band

If the instrument has a reference_price and a non-zero price_band, limit order prices must not deviate from the reference price by more than the band percentage:

|price - ref_price| / ref_price ≤ price_band

This protects against fat-finger orders far from the market. The price_band is a decimal fraction — e.g. 0.20 means ±20%.

Fails with: PriceBandViolation

Check 8: Fat finger protection

A second, tighter price deviation check using the per-instrument risk.fat_finger_pct field (a percentage, e.g. 5.0 for ±5%). This is independent of the price band check in #7 and is the primary fat-finger guard. The fat finger check uses reference_price defaulting to 1.0 if unset.

Fails with: FatFingerProtection

Check 9: Participant active

The participant config’s active field must be true. Deactivated participants cannot submit orders.

Fails with: ParticipantNotActive

Note: In the PretradeActor, this check is also performed at the actor level (calling self.participant_registry.get(...) first, then checking !p.active) before the check_order function runs. The check_order function also performs this check redundantly for non-actor callers.

Check 10: Notional limit

If the instrument config has risk.max_notional_per_order set, the order’s notional value (qty × price) must not exceed it. This limits the gross exposure of a single order.

Fails with: NotionalLimitExceeded

Check 11: Position limit

If the participant config has position_limits entries for the instrument, the worst-case position after the order must not exceed the limit. The check computes:

  • Buy: current_position + qty ≤ limit
  • Sell: current_position - qty ≥ -limit (i.e. |new_position| ≤ limit)

Current position is read live from the participant’s ledger (tracked by the PretradeActor’s background event subscriber).

Fails with: PositionLimitExceeded

Duplicate ClOrdID protection

The PretradeActor maintains a HashSet<(participant, instrument, client_order_id)> of all currently active order IDs. This set is updated from the event stream:

  • OrderAccepted → insert the key.
  • OrderCancelled → remove the key.
  • OrderReplaced → insert the new client_order_id key.

If a new order arrives with a (participant, instrument, client_order_id) tuple already in the set, it is rejected with DuplicateClOrdId. This prevents accidental duplicate submissions, including across gateways (the same ClOrdID from FIX or WS is caught).

Rejection flow

When any check fails:

  1. check_order() returns a RiskCheckResult { valid: false, reason: Some(...) }.
  2. The PretradeActor::process_command() method constructs an ExchangeEvent::OrderRejected event with the specific RejectReason.
  3. The event is wrapped in a SequencedEvent and broadcast on event_tx.
  4. The order command is not forwarded to the engine channel. The matching engine never sees it.

All gateways listening on event_tx receive the rejection event and can relay it to the originating client (ExecutionReport or ServerMessage).

Pass-through commands

Only OrderCommand::New orders go through the full 11-point pipeline. All other commands bypass pretrade checks and are forwarded to the engine unchanged:

CommandTreatment
OrderCommand::NewFull 11-point check
OrderCommand::CancelPass-through
OrderCommand::ReplacePass-through
OrderCommand::StatusPass-through
OrderCommand::ClockTickPass-through
OrderCommand::SessionTransitionPass-through
OrderCommand::ScenarioPass-through

The PretradeActor maps these at process_command() (actor.rs:135-142): only OrderCommand::New(fields) is destructured for risk checking; everything else returns PretradeResult::Forward.

Defense-in-depth

The matching engine (crates/nadatrade-matching/src/engine.rs:649-722) retains a second layer of validation as a safety net, even though orders have already passed pretrade:

  • Session state gating — with full order-type and TIF restrictions per session (pre-open accepts Day/Gtc/Gtd/AtOpen limit orders; opening auction accepts limit orders only; trading accepts everything; reopening auction accepts Day/Gtc/Gtd limit orders; closing auction accepts Day/Gtc/Gtd/AtClose limit orders).
  • TIF compatibility — calls validate_tif() from the 42-cell TIF matrix.
  • Lot size — redundant with check #5.
  • Min/max qty — redundant with check #4.
  • Tick size — redundant with check #6.
  • Price band — redundant with check #7.

These engine checks use the same instrument config directly. They are a defense-in-depth layer: if pretrade is bypassed, misconfigured, or replaced, the engine still refuses invalid orders.

Configuration

Per-instrument (TOML, instruments/ directory)

[instruments.PETR4]
name = "PETR4"
currency = "BRL"
tick_size = "0.01"
lot_size = "100"
min_qty = "1"
max_qty = "1000000"
price_band = "0.20"             # ±20% band from reference_price
reference_price = "38.50"       # optional; if unset, band/fat-finger checks are skipped

[instruments.PETR4.risk]
fat_finger_pct = 5.0            # ±5% fat finger threshold
max_notional_per_order = "500000"  # notional limit (optional)

The risk block is RiskConfig (instrument.rs:116-126):

FieldTypeDefaultDescription
fat_finger_pctf640.0Percentage deviation threshold for fat-finger rejections.
max_notional_per_orderDecimal (optional)NoneMaximum notional (qty × price) per order.

Per-participant (TOML, participants/ directory)

[participants.BOT001]
comp_id = "BOT001"
display_name = "Trading Bot 1"
active = true
gtc_eod_purge = false

[participants.BOT001.position_limits]
PETR4 = "10000"
VALE3 = "5000"
FieldTypeDefaultDescription
activebooltrueWhether the participant can submit orders.
position_limitsmap{}Per-instrument position cap (long/short absolute).

Global matching config (TOML, config/nadatrade.toml)

[matching]
book_depth_levels = 20

The global [matching] section currently configures book depth. Fat finger, price band, notional, and position limits are all per-instrument or per-participant settings.

Related crates

CrateRole

See Also

  • Matching Engine — defense-in-depth validation in the engine
  • Settlement — how position limits interact with the ledger
