Logging

Configure in config/nadatrade.toml:

[logging]
level = "info"       # trace, debug, info, warn, error
format = "pretty"    # pretty, json, compact, full

Formats

pretty (default) — human-readable, multi-line with timestamps:

2026-06-11T14:30:00.123Z  INFO nadatrade_bin: Starting exchange on 0.0.0.0:9824

json — single-line JSON for log aggregation (ELK, Loki, Datadog):

{"timestamp":"2026-06-11T14:30:00.123Z","level":"INFO","target":"nadatrade_bin","message":"Starting exchange on 0.0.0.0:9824"}

Use json format when shipping logs to a central aggregator.

Key Log Events

PatternMeaning
Starting exchange on ...Server boot, gateways listening
Recovery loaded N eventsReplay completed after restart
Partition created: events_yYYYYMMNew monthly events partition
Snapshot written for ...Per-instrument snapshot persisted
Queue depth warning: N > warn_depthPersist buffer backing up
Lag detected, N events droppedEvent consumer can’t keep up

TUI as a Monitoring Tool

The TUI dashboard (TUI Dashboard) provides real-time visual monitoring for operators logged into the server:

  • Instruments tab — session state color-coded at a glance (green=trading, red=halted)
  • Order Book tab — live depth, spot thin books before they cause problems
  • Events tab — tail the event log, watch for rejection storms
  • Clock tab — clock speed, sim time, pause status
  • Ledger tab — participant positions and P&L

Launch with [tui] enabled = true in config or run cargo run -p nadatrade-admin from the server.

Security

Hardening a NadaTrade deployment for multi-tenant remote access. Every section below applies when the exchange is reachable over a network.

JWT Authentication

All WebSocket and HTTP connections authenticate with HS256 JWT tokens. The token carries the participant identity (sub claim) and an expiry (exp claim).

Token Structure

{
  "sub": "ALICE",
  "exp": 1751328000,
  "role": "admin"     // optional; only needed for Admin API
}

The sub claim is the participant comp_id — it must match a configured participant in your registry.

Generating Tokens

The secret is set in config/nadatrade.toml:

ws_jwt_secret = "env:NADATRADE_JWT_SECRET"

Generate tokens for your users:

import jwt, time

secret = "your-secret-from-env"
token = jwt.encode(
    {"sub": "ALICE", "exp": int(time.time()) + 31536000},  # 1 year
    secret, algorithm="HS256"
)
print(token)

Or with the CLI:

python3 -c "
import jwt, time
print(jwt.encode({'sub':'ALICE','exp':int(time.time())+31536000}, '$SECRET', algorithm='HS256'))
"

Token Distribution

Give each bot developer their token securely (password manager, 1Password shared vault, or encrypted chat). Tokens are bearer credentials — anyone with a valid token can trade as that participant.

Token Rotation

To rotate the secret:

  1. Update NADATRADE_JWT_SECRET in your environment
  2. Restart nadatrade
  3. Generate new tokens for all participants with the new secret
  4. Distribute new tokens; old tokens stop working immediately after restart

Admin Tokens

Admin API access requires a separate token with "role": "admin":

token = jwt.encode(
    {"sub": "admin", "role": "admin", "exp": int(time.time()) + 3600},
    secret, algorithm="HS256"
)

Keep admin tokens short-lived (1-8 hours). Never share admin tokens with bot developers.

FIX Authentication

FIX sessions authenticate via username/password in the Logon (35=A) message using custom tags 553 (Username) and 554 (Password).

Configuring Credentials

FIX credentials are checked against a HashMap<String, String> on the FixSession. When credentials are configured, logon is rejected for unknown usernames or wrong passwords. When credentials are empty, logon is rejected unless allow_no_auth is explicitly set to true.

In code (from nadatrade-fix):

#![allow(unused)]
fn main() {
// Dev mode — must opt in
session.credentials = HashMap::new();
session.allow_no_auth = true;

// Production — require credentials
session.credentials = {
    "alice".into() => "alice-pass".into(),
    "bob".into()   => "bob-fix-key".into(),
};
session.allow_no_auth = false; // default
}

Password Best Practices

  • Use randomly generated strings (not dictionary words)
  • Store credentials in environment variables, never in code
  • Rotate passwords periodically
  • The FIX password is sent in plaintext over the TCP connection — use stunnel or VPN to encrypt the transport (see Deployment)

Network Security

Binding to Specific Interfaces

By default, both gateways bind to 0.0.0.0 (all interfaces). For a server with both public and private NICs, restrict to internal interfaces:

[server]
fix_listen_addr = "10.0.0.5:9823"    # internal network only
ws_listen_addr = "10.0.0.5:9824"

Firewall Rules

Minimum required ports:

# Allow client connections
ufw allow from 10.0.0.0/24 to any port 9823 proto tcp  # FIX
ufw allow from 10.0.0.0/24 to any port 9824 proto tcp  # WS+HTTP

# Or restrict to specific IPs
ufw allow from 10.0.0.10 to any port 9823 proto tcp

Block everything else:

ufw default deny incoming
ufw default allow outgoing

Never expose PostgreSQL (port 5432) to the internet.

Rate Limiting

NadaTrade does not include built-in rate limiting. For production multi-tenant use, add rate limiting at the reverse proxy level:

nginx rate limiting example:

limit_req_zone $binary_remote_addr zone=nadatrade:10m rate=50r/s;

server {
    location /v1/orders {
        limit_req zone=nadatrade burst=20 nodelay;
        proxy_pass http://127.0.0.1:9824;
        # ... websocket upgrade headers
    }
}

TLS Encryption

NadaTrade does not implement TLS natively. Choose one of three options:

1. Reverse Proxy (HTTPS/WSS)

Terminate TLS at nginx/Caddy. Clients use wss:// for WebSocket connections. This is the recommended approach. See Deployment for config examples.

2. stunnel (FIX TLS)

Wrap the FIX TCP port with stunnel for encrypted FIX sessions. Clients connect to the stunnel port instead of the raw FIX port.

3. VPN / WireGuard

Run the exchange on a private network and require all bot developers to connect via VPN. This avoids TLS complexity entirely and encrypts all traffic.

Participant Isolation

What can one participant see about another?

DataVisibility
Order book depthPublic — all participants see the full aggregated book
Trade printsPublic — price, quantity, aggressor side (not who traded)
Session statePublic — everyone sees state transitions
Own fillsPrivate — only the participant who submitted the order
Own rejectsPrivate — rejection reasons are not broadcast
Other participants’ fillsHidden — fills are filtered by participant identity
Other participants’ positionsHidden — ledger data is per-participant
Admin endpointsRequires admin JWT token with "role": "admin"

The WebSocket gateway filters events by participant ID before sending. The FIX gateway filters by client_order_id and participant tracking.

Environment Variables & Secrets

Using env: Prefix

All config values support the env:VARNAME syntax. The variable is read at startup:

ws_jwt_secret = "env:NADATRADE_JWT_SECRET"

[database]
url = "env:DATABASE_URL"

What Must Be Secret

Config keySensitivityStorage
ws_jwt_secretSecretEnvironment variable only; never in TOML
database.urlSecret — contains credentialsEnvironment variable or restricted TOML
FIX credentials mapSecret — contains passwordsEnvironment variable or compiled-in
Everything elseNot secret — can be in TOMLConfig file in version control

Docker Secrets

In Docker Compose, use the .env file (in .gitignore):

# .env
NADATRADE_JWT_SECRET=<random-hex>
DATABASE_URL=postgres://nadatrade:<password>@postgres:5432/nadatrade

For Kubernetes, use Secrets and mount them as environment variables. For systemd, use EnvironmentFile=/etc/nadatrade/env with mode 0600.

Checklist

  • ws_jwt_secret is set via environment variable (never hardcoded)
  • Database password is not in the config TOML file
  • .env file is in .gitignore and not committed
  • Admin tokens are short-lived (hours, not years)
  • Bot developer tokens are distributed securely
  • FIX connections are encrypted via stunnel or VPN
  • Firewall allows only ports 9823 and 9824 (not PostgreSQL)
  • /healthz and /metrics are not publicly exposed (or are behind auth)

Troubleshooting & FAQ

Common problems and how to solve them, plus frequently asked questions.

Troubleshooting

Startup Issues

PostgreSQL connection refused

Error: error connecting to database: Connection refused (os error 111)
  • Is PostgreSQL running? systemctl status postgresql or docker ps
  • Is database.url correct? Check host/port/dbname in config
  • Is the PostgreSQL host reachable? telnet <host> 5432
  • In Docker: use service name postgres not localhost in the URL

Port already in use

Error: Address already in use (os error 98)
  • Another nadatrade or other process is using port 9823 or 9824
  • Find it: ss -tlnp | grep -E '98(23|24)'
  • Kill it or change fix_listen_addr / ws_listen_addr in config

Missing config file

Error: No such file or directory (os error 2)
  • Pass the config path: --config config/nadatrade.toml
  • Verify the file exists: ls config/nadatrade.toml

Connection Issues

FIX Logon rejected

A SessionReject (35=3) with 58=Invalid credentials means:

  • Username (553) doesn’t match any configured participant
  • Password (554) is wrong
  • Credentials map is configured but the credentials don’t match

A SessionReject (35=3) with 58=Session not logged on; send Logon first means:

  • You sent an order before logging on
  • Always send Logon (35=A) first

A SessionReject (35=3) with 58=Unknown message type means:

  • You sent a message type the gateway doesn’t recognize
  • Check your FIX message type codes

WebSocket 401 Unauthorized

  • JWT token missing or invalid
  • Token expired (check exp claim)
  • Secret doesn’t match the server’s ws_jwt_secret
  • Token not passed correctly (query param ?token= or Authorization: Bearer header)
  • Participant in sub claim doesn’t exist in registry

WebSocket 403 Forbidden

  • Valid token, but admin endpoints require "role": "admin" in the JWT claims
  • Regular participant tokens cannot access /v1/admin/*

Connection drops immediately

  • For FIX: CheckSum (10) or BodyLength (9) is wrong — the message is malformed
  • For FIX: Sequence number (34) gap detected — server sent ResendRequest
  • For WS: Network timeout, check your heartbeat/ping interval

Order Issues

“My order was rejected — how do I find out why?”

Check the rejection reason in the response:

  • FIX: ExecutionReport (35=8) with 39=8 and 58=<reason>
  • WS: order_rejected message with reason field

Common causes:

ReasonWhat to check
InvalidQuantityQty ≤ 0, below min_qty, above max_qty, not a multiple of lot_size
InvalidPricePrice ≤ 0, not a multiple of tick_size, wrong field for order type
PriceBandViolationPrice too far from reference_price ± price_band
FatFingerProtectionPrice deviation exceeds risk.fat_finger_pct
SessionNotAcceptingOrdersInstrument is Closed or order type not allowed in current state
InstrumentHaltedCircuit breaker or manual halt active
UnknownInstrumentInstrument ID not in registry
UnknownParticipantParticipant not configured
ParticipantNotActiveParticipant has active = false
NotionalLimitExceededqty × price exceeds risk.max_notional_per_order
PositionLimitExceededOrder would exceed participant’s position limit
DuplicateClOrdIdYou already have an active order with this client_order_id
IncompatibleOrderTypeAndTifThe order type and TIF combination is invalid (e.g., Market+GTC)
MarketOrderNoLiquidityMarket order but no opposite-side resting orders exist
StpCancelledSelf-trade prevention cancelled your order

“My order was accepted but never filled”

  • Is the instrument in Trading state? Check session state.
  • Is your price crossing the opposite side? A buy at 38.50 won’t fill if the best ask is at 38.55.
  • Are you trying to match against your own orders? STP may cancel them.
  • Is the order type correct? Stop orders rest in the Stop Book until triggered; they won’t fill until the trigger price is reached.

“My GTD order didn’t expire when I expected”

  • GTD expiry uses sim_time, not wall clock. In accelerated or replay mode the clock may not align with real time.
  • The expire_time format in FIX is YYYYMMDD-HH:MM:SS (UTC).
  • GTD stops in the Stop Book are checked on every ClockTick, not continuously.

Persistence Issues

“Snapshots are not being created”

  • Check persist.snapshot_every_events in config (default 10000)
  • Only events with an instrument_id increment the counter
  • Low-volume instruments will take longer to reach the threshold
  • Verify PostgreSQL is writable (check logs for write errors)

“Recovery is taking too long after restart”

  • Decrease persist.snapshot_every_events to take more frequent snapshots
  • Each snapshot means fewer events to replay
  • The snapshots table keeps the 3 most recent per instrument; prune older ones
  • Very large event tables with no snapshots will replay from sequence 0 — this can take minutes or hours

“Events are lagging / queue depth warning”

  • persist.queue_warn_depth triggers a warning when the in-memory buffer grows
  • Increase persist.batch_max or decrease persist.batch_max_ms to flush more aggressively
  • Check PostgreSQL performance: slow disks, network latency, or competing queries

TUI Issues

“TUI not appearing”

  • Set [tui] enabled = true in config
  • If running standalone: cargo run -p nadatrade-admin (must be in-process)
  • Ensure terminal supports alternate screen mode

“Terminal messed up after TUI exit”

  • Run reset or stty sane
  • This happens if the process was killed with SIGKILL (not Ctrl+C)
  • Ctrl+C sends SIGTERM which the TUI catches and restores the terminal

FAQ

General

Can I run NadaTrade without PostgreSQL?

Yes. Omit the [database] section from config and the system runs entirely in-memory. No data survives a restart.

How do I completely reset state?

# Docker
docker compose down -v && docker compose up -d

# Bare metal — drop and recreate the database
psql -c "DROP DATABASE nadatrade; CREATE DATABASE nadatrade;"

How do I add a new instrument?

Add a TOML file in instruments/ (hot-reloaded), or use the Admin API:

curl -X POST http://<host>:9824/v1/admin/instruments \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $ADMIN_TOKEN" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"name":"NEWCO","currency":"BRL","tick_size":"0.01","lot_size":"100","session_type":"session_based","settlement_currency":"BRL"}'

What happens when I restart?

  1. PostgreSQL: events and snapshots are replayed to rebuild engine state
  2. Without PostgreSQL: start fresh with empty books

Open orders (GTC/GTD) survive restarts if persistence is enabled.

Can multiple bots share the same participant ID?

Yes, if you set StpMode::None on every order. Otherwise, orders from the same participant will self-trade-prevent each other. For isolation, give each bot its own participant ID.

What ports do my bot developers need access to?

9823 (FIX TCP) and/or 9824 (WebSocket + HTTP). They do not need PostgreSQL access.

Simulation

How do I simulate a market crash / flash crash?

Use scenario injection. From the TUI: select an instrument, press s to trigger a circuit breaker, or h to halt. Via API: send a scenario message.

How do I fast-forward to see next week’s settlement?

Enable accelerated mode:

[simulation]
mode = "accelerated"
clock_speed = 3600.0    # 1 wall second = 1 sim hour

Can I replay historical market data?

Yes. Put your data in NDJSON format (see Simulation) and configure replay mode:

[simulation]
mode = "replay"
replay_feed = "/data/nyse_2024-01-15.ndjson"

Matching

What’s the difference between price_band and fat_finger_pct?

  • price_band — instrument-level guard (±X% from reference_price). Used for price band validation and circuit breaker thresholds.
  • risk.fat_finger_pct — tighter per-instrument guard (±Y% from last trade or reference). Catches accidental keystroke errors.
  • Both must pass. Typically fat_finger_pct is smaller than price_band.

How do auctions work?

See Sessions & Auctions. Auctions collect orders during a specified window (PreOpen, OpeningAuction, ClosingAuction), then uncross() runs a call-market algorithm to find a single clearing price that maximizes traded volume.

Can I cancel an order during a trading halt?

Yes. Cancels and replaces are allowed in TradingHalt. Only new orders are blocked.

Configuration

How do I use environment variables in config?

Prefix values with env::

[database]
url = "env:DATABASE_URL"
ws_jwt_secret = "env:NADATRADE_JWT_SECRET"

The env: prefix is stripped, the remaining string is looked up as an environment variable. If not set, the value resolves to empty string (or None for optional keys).

What log levels should I use?

LevelWhen
errorProduction — only failures
warnProduction — problems that don’t break things
infoDefault — key lifecycle events (recovery, snapshots, connections)
debugDevelopment — per-message detail
traceDeep debugging — every channel send/receive

Building Bots: Python

Connect to NadaTrade from Python using either FIX 4.2 (raw TCP) or WebSocket (JSON). Both gateways produce identical order flow — the choice is about wire format and library availability, not capability.

Choosing a Gateway

FIX 4.2WebSocket
TransportRaw TCP, port 9823WebSocket, port 9824
AuthLogon (35=A) with username/passwordJWT HS256 token as query param
FramingSOH-delimited tag=value, BodyLength, CheckSumJSON text frames
LibraryStandard library only (socket)websockets (pip install websockets)
Best forExisting FIX infrastructure, lowest latencyQuick prototyping, browser clients
Market data35=V (subscribe), 35=W/X (snapshots/updates)subscribe/unsubscribe messages

Prerequisites

  • Python 3.9+
  • NadaTrade running on your server (or localhost)
  • FIX track: no dependencies beyond standard library
  • WS track: pip install websockets
  • JWT generation (WS): pip install pyjwt (only needed to generate tokens; not for the bot itself)

Track A: FIX 4.2 Bot

Connection Lifecycle

Every FIX session follows this sequence:

TCP connect → Logon (35=A) → Orders (35=D/F/G) → Logout (35=5) → TCP close

Until Logon succeeds, any non-A message is rejected with a SessionReject (35=3).

FIX Message Framing

Every FIX message is structured as:

8=FIX.4.2\x019=<body-length>\x01<tag=value pairs ending with \x01>10=<checksum>\x01
  • SOH (\x01) is the field delimiter
  • BodyLength (tag 9) counts bytes from tag 35 to the delimiter before tag 10
  • CheckSum (tag 10) sums all preceding bytes modulo 256, zero-padded to 3 digits

Top 20 FIX Tags

TagNamePurpose
8BeginStringAlways FIX.4.2
9BodyLengthByte length of message body
10CheckSumMod-256 checksum of preceding bytes
34MsgSeqNumSequence number (starts at 1)
35MsgTypeA=Logon, D=NewOrder, F=Cancel, G=Replace, 8=ExecReport, 5=Logout
49SenderCompIDYour identifier
56TargetCompIDServer identifier (usually NADATRADE)
11ClOrdIDYour order reference ID
41OrigClOrdIDOriginal order ID for cancel/replace
37OrderIDExchange-assigned order UUID
55SymbolInstrument ID (e.g. PETR4)
54Side1=Buy, 2=Sell
38OrderQtyQuantity
40OrdType1=Market, 2=Limit, 3=Stop, 4=StopLimit, K=MarketToLimit
44PriceLimit price
99StopPxStop trigger price
59TimeInForce0=Day, 1=GTC, 3=IOC, 4=FOK, 6=GTD
39OrdStatus0=New, 1=Partial, 2=Filled, 4=Cancelled, 8=Rejected
150ExecType0=New, 1=Partial, 2=Filled, 4=Cancelled, 8=Rejected
14CumQtyCumulative filled quantity

See FIX Message Catalog for the complete 50+ tag reference.

Helper Functions

import socket, datetime

SOH = "\x01"

def checksum(msg):
    """Sum all bytes modulo 256."""
    return sum(msg.encode("ascii")) % 256

def fix_msg(body):
    """Wrap body with header, BodyLength, and CheckSum."""
    msg = f"8=FIX.4.2{SOH}9={len(body)}{SOH}{body}"
    return f"{msg}10={checksum(msg):03d}{SOH}"

def recv_fix(sock):
    """Read one FIX message delimited by trailing SOH."""
    buf = b""
    while not buf.endswith(b"\x01"):
        chunk = sock.recv(4096)
        if not chunk:
            return None
        buf += chunk
    return buf.decode("ascii")

def parse_tags(msg):
    """Parse FIX tag=value pairs into a dict."""
    return dict(pair.split("=", 1) for pair in msg.split(SOH) if "=" in pair)

Logon

sock = socket.socket(socket.AF_INET, socket.SOCK_STREAM)
sock.connect(("your-server.example.com", 9823))

now = datetime.datetime.utcnow().strftime("%Y%m%d-%H:%M:%S")
logon_body = (
    f"35=A{SOH}"
    f"49=MY-BOT{SOH}"
    f"56=NADATRADE{SOH}"
    f"34=1{SOH}"
    f"52={now}{SOH}"
    f"98=0{SOH}"
    f"108=30{SOH}"
    f"553=alice{SOH}"       # your FIX username
    f"554=alice-pass{SOH}"  # your FIX password
)
sock.sendall(fix_msg(logon_body).encode("ascii"))

resp = parse_tags(recv_fix(sock))
assert resp.get("35") == "A", f"Logon failed: {resp.get('58', 'unknown error')}"
print(f"Connected: heartbeat={resp.get('108')}s")

Sending Orders

Limit order:

order_body = (
    f"35=D{SOH}49=MY-BOT{SOH}56=NADATRADE{SOH}34=2{SOH}"
    f"11=order-1{SOH}55=PETR4{SOH}54=1{SOH}"
    f"38=100{SOH}40=2{SOH}44=50.00{SOH}59=1{SOH}"
)
sock.sendall(fix_msg(order_body).encode("ascii"))

Market order:

mkt_body = (
    f"35=D{SOH}49=MY-BOT{SOH}56=NADATRADE{SOH}34=3{SOH}"
    f"11=order-2{SOH}55=PETR4{SOH}54=1{SOH}"
    f"38=100{SOH}40=1{SOH}59=3{SOH}"   # Market, IOC
)
sock.sendall(fix_msg(mkt_body).encode("ascii"))

Stop order:

stop_body = (
    f"35=D{SOH}49=MY-BOT{SOH}56=NADATRADE{SOH}34=4{SOH}"
    f"11=order-3{SOH}55=PETR4{SOH}54=2{SOH}"
    f"38=500{SOH}40=3{SOH}99=68.00{SOH}59=1{SOH}"
)
sock.sendall(fix_msg(stop_body).encode("ascii"))

Iceberg order (display 100 of 1000):

ice_body = (
    f"35=D{SOH}49=MY-BOT{SOH}56=NADATRADE{SOH}34=5{SOH}"
    f"11=order-4{SOH}55=PETR4{SOH}54=1{SOH}"
    f"38=1000{SOH}40=2{SOH}44=38.50{SOH}59=1{SOH}"
    f"210=100{SOH}"    # MaxFloor = display quantity
)
sock.sendall(fix_msg(ice_body).encode("ascii"))

Cancelling an Order

cancel_body = (
    f"35=F{SOH}49=MY-BOT{SOH}56=NADATRADE{SOH}34=6{SOH}"
    f"11=cxl-1{SOH}41=order-1{SOH}55=PETR4{SOH}54=1{SOH}"
)
sock.sendall(fix_msg(cancel_body).encode("ascii"))

Replacing an Order

replace_body = (
    f"35=G{SOH}49=MY-BOT{SOH}56=NADATRADE{SOH}34=7{SOH}"
    f"11=order-1v2{SOH}41=order-1{SOH}55=PETR4{SOH}54=1{SOH}"
    f"38=200{SOH}44=51.00{SOH}"
)
sock.sendall(fix_msg(replace_body).encode("ascii"))

Reading Execution Reports

def read_responses(sock, count=3):
    """Read and display ExecutionReports."""
    for _ in range(count):
        msg = recv_fix(sock)
        tags = parse_tags(msg)
        msg_type = tags.get("35")
        if msg_type == "8":  # ExecutionReport
            print(f"  ER: exec_type={tags.get('150')} "
                  f"ord_status={tags.get('39')} "
                  f"cum={tags.get('14')} leaves={tags.get('151')} "
                  f"text={tags.get('58', '')}")
        elif msg_type == "9":  # CancelReject
            print(f"  CancelRejected: {tags.get('58')}")
        elif msg_type == "0":  # Heartbeat
            pass  # ignore heartbeats
        else:
            print(f"  MsgType={msg_type}: {tags}")

read_responses(sock, 5)

Logout

logout_body = (
    f"35=5{SOH}49=MY-BOT{SOH}56=NADATRADE{SOH}34=8{SOH}"
)
sock.sendall(fix_msg(logout_body).encode("ascii"))
sock.close()

Market Data Subscription (FIX)

# Subscribe to PETR4 order book
md_body = (
    f"35=V{SOH}49=MY-BOT{SOH}56=NADATRADE{SOH}34=9{SOH}"
    f"262=MDREQ-1{SOH}55=PETR4{SOH}263=1{SOH}"
)
sock.sendall(fix_msg(md_body).encode("ascii"))

# You'll receive 35=W (snapshot) and/or 35=X (incremental) messages

Track B: WebSocket Bot

JWT Token

The WebSocket gateway authenticates with HS256 JWT tokens. You need a token with the sub claim set to your participant comp_id:

import jwt, time

secret = "your-server-jwt-secret"  # same as ws_jwt_secret in server config
token = jwt.encode(
    {"sub": "ALICE", "exp": int(time.time()) + 31536000},
    secret, algorithm="HS256"
)

Pass the token as a query parameter: ws://host:9824/v1/orders?token=<token>.

Install Dependencies

pip install websockets

Connect and Send an Order

import asyncio, json
import websockets

TOKEN = "<your-jwt-token>"
HOST = "your-server.example.com"

async def main():
    uri = f"ws://{HOST}:9824/v1/orders?token={TOKEN}"

    async with websockets.connect(uri) as ws:
        # Server sends a welcome message first
        welcome = json.loads(await ws.recv())
        print(f"Connected: {welcome['message']}")

        # Send a limit order
        order = {
            "type": "new_order",
            "req_id": "r1",
            "instrument": "PETR4",
            "side": "buy",
            "order_type": "limit",
            "qty": "100",
            "price": "50.00",
            "tif": "day",
            "client_order_id": "my-order-1",
        }
        await ws.send(json.dumps(order))

        # Read responses
        while True:
            msg = json.loads(await ws.recv())
            t = msg["type"]
            print(f"  {t} (seq={msg.get('seq')})")

            if t == "fill" and msg.get("is_final"):
                break
            if t == "order_rejected":
                print(f"  Rejected: {msg['reason']}")
                break

asyncio.run(main())

Client Message Types

typePurposeKey Fields
new_orderSubmit orderinstrument, side, order_type, qty
cancel_orderCancel orderinstrument, orig_client_order_id
replace_orderModify orderinstrument, orig_client_order_id, new_qty/new_price
order_statusQuery order stateorder_id
subscribeMarket data subinstrument, channel
unsubscribeMarket data unsubinstrument, channel

All decimal values (prices, quantities) are JSON strings — never numbers. This avoids floating-point precision loss.

See WebSocket Protocol for every field and WebSocket Gateway for server response types.

Handling the Response Stream

Every server message (except error and unsubscribed) carries seq and sim_time. The seq field is a global monotonic counter — use it to order events across instruments.

async def handle_responses(ws):
    while True:
        msg = json.loads(await ws.recv())
        t = msg["type"]

        handlers = {
            "order_accepted": lambda m: print(f"Accepted: {m['order_id']}",
            "order_rejected": lambda m: print(f"Rejected: {m['reason']}"),
            "fill": lambda m: print(f"Fill: {m['fill_qty']} @ {m['fill_price']} {'FINAL' if m['is_final'] else 'partial'}"),
            "order_cancelled": lambda m: print(f"Cancelled: {m['reason']}"),
            "order_expired": lambda m: print(f"Expired"),
            "order_replaced": lambda m: print(f"Replaced: {m['order_id']} -> {m['new_order_id']}"),
            "cancel_rejected": lambda m: print(f"Cancel rejected: {m['reason']}"),
            "error": lambda m: print(f"Error: {m['code']} — {m['message']}"),
        }

        handler = handlers.get(t)
        if handler:
            handler(msg)

Market Data Subscriptions

async def market_data_example():
    uri = f"ws://{HOST}:9824/v1/marketdata?token={TOKEN}"

    async with websockets.connect(uri) as ws:
        welcome = json.loads(await ws.recv())

        # Subscribe to trades and orderbook
        await ws.send(json.dumps({
            "type": "subscribe", "req_id": "r1",
            "instrument": "PETR4", "channel": "trades"
        }))
        await ws.send(json.dumps({
            "type": "subscribe", "req_id": "r2",
            "instrument": "PETR4", "channel": "orderbook"
        }))

        while True:
            msg = json.loads(await ws.recv())
            t = msg["type"]

            if t == "trade":
                print(f"Trade: {msg['instrument']} @ {msg['price']} qty={msg['qty']} side={msg['side']}")
            elif t == "orderbook_snapshot":
                print(f"Book: bids={msg['bids'][:3]} asks={msg['asks'][:3]}")
            elif t == "orderbook_update":
                print(f"Book update: bids={msg['bids']} asks={msg['asks']} deletes={msg['deletes']}")
            elif t == "session":
                print(f"Session: {msg['instrument']} -> {msg['state']}")

Error Handling Patterns

async def safe_send(ws, msg):
    """Send with error handling for server-side errors."""
    await ws.send(json.dumps(msg))

    # The immediate ack for new/cancel/replace comes as an error-type
    # with codes like "cancel_sent" or "replace_sent"
    resp = json.loads(await ws.recv())
    if resp["type"] == "error":
        if resp["code"] in ("cancel_sent", "replace_sent"):
            return  # These are expected acks, not errors
        raise RuntimeError(f"Server error: {resp['code']} — {resp['message']}")
    return resp

Complete Bot Template

Save as bot.py:

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""NadaTrade WebSocket bot template."""
import asyncio, json, os
import websockets

HOST = os.environ.get("NADATRADE_HOST", "localhost")
TOKEN = os.environ.get("NADATRADE_TOKEN", "your-token")

async def run():
    uri = f"ws://{HOST}:9824/v1/orders?token={TOKEN}"

    async with websockets.connect(uri) as ws:
        welcome = json.loads(await ws.recv())
        print(welcome["message"])

        # --- Your strategy here ---

        await ws.send(json.dumps({
            "type": "new_order",
            "req_id": "r1",
            "instrument": os.environ.get("INSTRUMENT", "PETR4"),
            "side": "buy",
            "order_type": "limit",
            "qty": "100",
            "price": "50.00",
            "tif": "day",
        }))

        async for raw in ws:
            msg = json.loads(raw)
            t = msg["type"]

            if t == "order_accepted":
                print(f"Accepted: {msg['order_id'][:8]}...")
            elif t == "fill":
                print(f"Fill: {msg['fill_qty']} @ {msg['fill_price']}")
                if msg.get("is_final"):
                    break
            elif t == "order_rejected":
                print(f"Rejected: {msg['reason']}")
                break

    print("Done.")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    asyncio.run(run())

Run with:

export NADATRADE_HOST=your-server.example.com
export NADATRADE_TOKEN="eyJ..."
export INSTRUMENT=PETR4
python bot.py

Building Bots: Rust

Connect to NadaTrade from Rust using tokio for async I/O. This guide covers TCP-based FIX 4.2 and WebSocket/JSON clients, plus how to use nadatrade-core types directly.

Prerequisites

  • Rust 1.78+
  • Add to Cargo.toml:
[dependencies]
tokio = { version = "1", features = ["full"] }
tokio-tungstenite = { version = "0.21", features = ["native-tls"] }
futures-util = "0.3"
serde = { version = "1", features = ["derive"] }
serde_json = "1"
rust_decimal = "1"
chrono = { version = "0.4", features = ["serde"] }
uuid = { version = "1", features = ["v4"] }

FIX 4.2 Client

Connection & Logon

use tokio::io::{AsyncBufReadExt, AsyncWriteExt, BufReader};
use tokio::net::TcpStream;

const SOH: char = '\x01';

fn checksum(msg: &str) -> u8 {
    (msg.as_bytes().iter().map(|&b| b as u32).sum::<u32>() % 256) as u8
}

fn fix_msg(body: &str) -> String {
    let header = format!("8=FIX.4.2{SOH}9={}{SOH}", body.len());
    let pre_checksum = format!("{}{}", header, body);
    format!("{}10={:03}{SOH}", pre_checksum, checksum(&pre_checksum))
}

#[tokio::main]
async fn main() -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    let mut stream = TcpStream::connect("your-server.example.com:9823").await?;

    // Logon
    let now = chrono::Utc::now().format("%Y%m%d-%H:%M:%S");
    let logon_body = format!(
        "35=A{SOH}49=MY-BOT{SOH}56=NADATRADE{SOH}34=1{SOH}\
         52={now}{SOH}98=0{SOH}108=30{SOH}\
         553=alice{SOH}554=alice-pass{SOH}"
    );
    stream.write_all(fix_msg(&logon_body).as_bytes()).await?;

    // Read logon response
    let (reader, mut writer) = stream.into_split();
    let mut buf_reader = BufReader::new(reader);
    let mut line = String::new();
    buf_reader.read_line(&mut line).await?;

    // ... proceed with orders
    Ok(())
}

Sending Orders & Reading Responses

#![allow(unused)]
fn main() {
// Send a limit order
let order_body = format!(
    "35=D{SOH}49=MY-BOT{SOH}56=NADATRADE{SOH}34=2{SOH}\
     11=order-1{SOH}55=PETR4{SOH}54=1{SOH}\
     38=100{SOH}40=2{SOH}44=50.00{SOH}59=1{SOH}"
);
writer.write_all(fix_msg(&order_body).as_bytes()).await?;

// Read ExecutionReports
let mut buf = vec![0u8; 4096];
loop {
    let n = buf_reader.read(&mut buf).await?;
    if n == 0 { break; }
    let msg = String::from_utf8_lossy(&buf[..n]);

    // Parse tag=value pairs
    let tags: std::collections::HashMap<&str, &str> = msg
        .split(SOH)
        .filter_map(|pair| pair.split_once('='))
        .collect();

    match tags.get("35") {
        Some(&"8") => {
            println!("ER: exec_type={} status={} cum={} leaves={}",
                tags.get("150").unwrap_or(&"?"),
                tags.get("39").unwrap_or(&"?"),
                tags.get("14").unwrap_or(&"?"),
                tags.get("151").unwrap_or(&"?"));
        }
        Some(&"0") => { /* heartbeat — ignore */ }
        Some(&"9") => {
            println!("CancelRejected: {}", tags.get("58").unwrap_or(&"?"));
            break;
        }
        _ => {}
    }
}

// Logout
let logout_body = format!(
    "35=5{SOH}49=MY-BOT{SOH}56=NADATRADE{SOH}34=3{SOH}"
);
writer.write_all(fix_msg(&logout_body).as_bytes()).await?;
}

WebSocket Client

Connect & Authenticate

use futures_util::{SinkExt, StreamExt};
use tokio_tungstenite::connect_async;
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};

#[derive(Debug, Serialize)]
#[serde(tag = "type")]
enum ClientMessage {
    #[serde(rename = "new_order")]
    NewOrder {
        req_id: String,
        instrument: String,
        side: String,
        order_type: String,
        qty: String,
        #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
        price: Option<String>,
        #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
        tif: Option<String>,
        #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
        client_order_id: Option<String>,
    },
    #[serde(rename = "cancel_order")]
    CancelOrder {
        req_id: String,
        instrument: String,
        orig_client_order_id: String,
    },
    #[serde(rename = "subscribe")]
    Subscribe {
        req_id: String,
        instrument: String,
        channel: String,
    },
}

#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
#[serde(tag = "type")]
enum ServerMessage {
    #[serde(rename = "order_accepted")]
    OrderAccepted {
        order_id: String,
        instrument: String,
        client_order_id: String,
        seq: u64,
        sim_time: String,
    },
    #[serde(rename = "fill")]
    Fill {
        order_id: String,
        fill_qty: String,
        fill_price: String,
        is_final: bool,
        seq: u64,
        sim_time: String,
    },
    #[serde(rename = "order_rejected")]
    OrderRejected {
        reason: String,
        seq: u64,
        sim_time: String,
    },
    #[serde(rename = "error")]
    Error {
        code: String,
        message: String,
    },
    // ... other variants as needed
}

#[tokio::main]
async fn main() -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    let token = "eyJ...your-jwt-token...";
    let url = format!("ws://your-server.example.com:9824/v1/orders?token={token}");

    let (mut ws, _) = connect_async(&url).await?;

    // Read welcome
    if let Some(Ok(msg)) = ws.next().await {
        let welcome: ServerMessage = serde_json::from_str(&msg.to_string())?;
        println!("Connected: {:?}", welcome);
    }

    // Send an order — note: all Decimal values are strings
    let order = serde_json::to_string(&ClientMessage::NewOrder {
        req_id: "r1".into(),
        instrument: "PETR4".into(),
        side: "buy".into(),
        order_type: "limit".into(),
        qty: "100".into(),
        price: Some("50.00".into()),
        tif: Some("day".into()),
        client_order_id: Some("my-order-1".into()),
    })?;
    ws.send(tokio_tungstenite::tungstenite::Message::Text(order)).await?;

    // Read responses
    while let Some(Ok(msg)) = ws.next().await {
        let text = msg.to_string();
        let event: ServerMessage = serde_json::from_str(&text)?;

        match event {
            ServerMessage::OrderAccepted { order_id, seq, .. } => {
                println!("Accepted: {order_id} (seq={seq})");
            }
            ServerMessage::Fill { fill_qty, fill_price, is_final, seq, .. } => {
                println!("Fill: {fill_qty} @ {fill_price} final={is_final} (seq={seq})");
                if is_final { break; }
            }
            ServerMessage::OrderRejected { reason, .. } => {
                println!("Rejected: {reason}");
                break;
            }
            ServerMessage::Error { code, message } => {
                if code != "cancel_sent" && code != "replace_sent" {
                    eprintln!("Error: {code} — {message}");
                }
            }
            _ => {}
        }
    }

    Ok(())
}

Market Data Streaming

#![allow(unused)]
fn main() {
let url = format!("ws://your-server.example.com:9824/v1/marketdata?token={token}");
let (mut ws, _) = connect_async(&url).await?;

// Subscribe
let sub = serde_json::to_string(&ClientMessage::Subscribe {
    req_id: "r1".into(),
    instrument: "PETR4".into(),
    channel: "trades".into(),
})?;
ws.send(tokio_tungstenite::tungstenite::Message::Text(sub)).await?;

// Read trade stream
while let Some(Ok(msg)) = ws.next().await {
    let text = msg.to_string();
    let v: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(&text)?;
    if v["type"] == "trade" {
        println!("Trade: {} @ {} qty={} side={}",
            v["instrument"], v["price"], v["qty"], v["side"]);
    }
}
}

Using nadatrade-core Types Directly

If your bot is a Rust binary, you can depend on nadatrade-core for type-safe enums and serde-compatible structs:

[dependencies]
nadatrade-core = { path = "../nadatrade/crates/nadatrade-core" }

#![allow(unused)]
fn main() {
use nadatrade_core::{Side, OrderType, TimeInForce};
use rust_decimal::Decimal;
use serde::Serialize;

#[derive(Serialize)]
struct MyOrder {
    #[serde(rename = "type")]
    msg_type: &'static str,  // "new_order"
    req_id: String,
    instrument: String,
    side: Side,              // serializes as "buy" / "sell"
    order_type: OrderType,   // serializes as "limit" / "market" / etc.
    qty: String,             // Decimal as string
    price: Option<String>,
    tif: TimeInForce,        // serializes as "day" / "gtc" / etc.
}
}

This gives you compile-time correctness for enum variants and ensures Decimal values are always serialized as strings.

Examples

NadaTrade ships with 6 example apps in examples/. Each demonstrates one or more features of the exchange. All examples are runnable with cargo run --example <name>.

Prerequisites

# From the nadatrade repo root
# Start the exchange first (in another terminal)
cargo run -p nadatrade-bin -- --config config/nadatrade.toml

All examples connect to localhost:9824 (WebSocket) or localhost:9823 (FIX) by default. Set environment variables to override:

NADATRADE_HOST=your-server.example.com cargo run --example basic_trade

basic_trade

What it does: Places a buy limit order and a sell limit order for the same instrument, demonstrating the full order lifecycle: accept → fill → done.

Run:

cargo run --example basic_trade

Expected output:

Connect to ws://localhost:9824/v1/orders?token=...
Welcome BOT001, you are connected to NadaTrade
Accepted: 550e8400-... (seq=1)
Order accepted: 550e8400-... (seq=2, from engine)
Fill: 100 @ 50.00 final=true (seq=3)

Concepts shown:

  • WebSocket connection with JWT auth
  • new_order message formatting (limit, day)
  • Order lifecycle event handling (accept → fill)
  • Order state tracking via order_id and is_final

fix_demo

What it does: Full FIX 4.2 session: TCP connect, Logon, NewOrderSingle, receive ExecutionReports, CancelRequest, Logout.

Run:

cargo run --example fix_demo

Expected output:

Connecting FIX to localhost:9823...
Logon sent
Logon accepted: heartbeat=30s
New order sent (ord-001)
ER: exec_type=0 status=0 (New) cum=0 leaves=100
ER: exec_type=1 status=1 (Partial) cum=50 leaves=50
ER: exec_type=2 status=2 (Filled) cum=100 leaves=0
Cancel sent (ord-001)
Logout sent
Disconnected.

Concepts shown:

  • Raw TCP connection
  • FIX message framing (BodyLength, CheckSum, SOH delimiter)
  • Logon authentication (35=A) with username/password
  • Order entry (35=D) and cancel (35=F)
  • ExecutionReport (35=8) parsing
  • Sequence number tracking (tag 34)

full_session

What it does: Simulates a complete trading day: PreOpen → OpeningAuction → Trading → ClosingAuction → Closed. Places AtOpen orders that uncross during the opening auction, then day orders during continuous trading, and AtClose orders during the closing auction.

Run:

cargo run --example full_session

Expected output:

Session: PreOpen
AtOpen limit buy 100 @ 50.00 accepted
Session: OpeningAuction
Auction uncrossed: clearing=50.00 qty=100
AtOpen order filled at auction
Session: Trading
Day order buy 50 @ 51.00 accepted
Day order filled
Session: ClosingAuction
AtClose limit sell 50 @ 51.00 accepted
Auction uncrossed: clearing=51.00
Session: Closed

Concepts shown:

  • Session state transitions and gating
  • AtOpen/AtClose TIF behavior
  • Auction call-market uncrossing
  • Day order EOD expiry (SessionEnd)

iceberg

What it does: Places an iceberg buy order (display 100, hidden 900) at 50.00 and a regular sell order for 300 at 50.00. Shows how the iceberg replenishes after visible portion is consumed, losing time priority.

Run:

cargo run --example iceberg

Expected output:

Iceberg order placed: display=100 hidden=900 @ 50.00
Sell order placed: qty=300 @ 50.00
First fill: iceberg 100 filled (display depleted)
Iceberg replenished: display=100 hidden=800 — re-enters at back of queue
Second fill: sell still filling against iceberg
...
Iceberg final: visible portion consumed, 600 remaining (hidden)
All fills complete

Concepts shown:

  • Iceberg order creation (display_qty field)
  • Visible vs hidden quantity tracking
  • Replenishment behavior (refills from hidden, loses time priority)
  • Multiple fills against the same iceberg

market_maker

What it does: Maintains a two-sided quote (bid + ask) for an instrument, demonstrating continuous quoting, position tracking, and spread management. Places limit orders on both sides, monitors fills, and replaces filled orders to maintain the quote.

Run:

cargo run --example market_maker

Expected output:

Market maker started: quoting PETR4 49.90 / 50.10 (spread 0.20)
Bid 100 @ 49.90 accepted
Ask 100 @ 50.10 accepted
Bid filled: 100 @ 49.90 (seq=5)
  Replenishing bid at 49.90
Ask partially filled: 50 @ 50.10 (seq=6)
  Leaves 50 @ 50.10 still resting
Quote status: bid 49.90(100) ask 50.10(50) position=+50

Concepts shown:

  • Continuous quoting pattern
  • Order lifecycle monitoring
  • Position tracking as quotes get filled
  • Spread management
  • Replacing filled orders to maintain quotes

stop_loss

What it does: Places a sell stop order at 48.00 when the market is at 50.00. When the last trade falls to 48.00 or below, the stop triggers and becomes a market order. Demonstrates stop order behavior and potential stop cascade.

Run:

cargo run --example stop_loss

Expected output:

Market at 50.00. Placing sell stop @ 48.00 (GTC)
Stop order parked in Stop Book (untriggered)
Trade: 49.50 — stop not yet triggered (49.50 > 48.00)
Trade: 48.00 — STOP TRIGGERED
Stop converted to market sell order
Stop fill: 100 @ 48.00
Stop cascade check: no further stops triggered

Concepts shown:

  • Stop order placement (stop_price field)
  • Stop Book storage (untriggered orders)
  • Trigger condition: last_trade_price <= stop_price for sell stops
  • Trigger → Market/Limit conversion
  • Stop cascade (breadth-first queue scanning after each fill)

Glossary & Cheat Sheet

Quick reference for exchange concepts, order types, FIX tags, and WebSocket message types.

Exchange Terminology

TermDefinition
CLOBCentral Limit Order Book — the data structure holding all resting orders at each price level
Price-time priorityOrders are matched first by best price, then by FIFO within a price level
AggressorThe incoming order that crosses the spread and matches against resting orders
Resting orderAn order already on the book waiting to be matched
BBOBest Bid and Offer — the highest bid and lowest ask currently on the book
SpreadThe gap between best bid and best ask (BBO). spread = ask - bid
Tick sizeMinimum price increment — all prices must be multiples
Lot sizeMinimum quantity increment — all quantities must be multiples
STPSelf-Trade Prevention — blocks a participant from trading against their own orders
IOCImmediate-or-Cancel — fill what you can now, cancel the rest
FOKFill-or-Kill — fill everything now or nothing at all
GTCGood-Till-Cancelled — survives session boundaries indefinitely
GTDGood-Till-Date — survives until a specific timestamp, then expires
UncrossThe auction algorithm that finds a single clearing price from collected orders
Market pressureThe imbalance between buy and sell demand at a candidate auction price
NotionalOrder gross value: qty × price
Mark-to-market (MtM)Current value of open positions at the last traded price

Order Types

Order TypeDescriptionRequiresTIF Allowed
MarketFill immediately at best available opposite priceqty, sideDay, IOC, FOK
LimitRest at a specified price until filled or cancelledqty, side, priceAll 7 TIFs
StopPark in Stop Book; trigger to Market when price is reachedqty, side, stop_priceDay, GTC, GTD
StopLimitLike Stop, but triggers into a Limit at the specified priceqty, side, price, stop_priceDay, GTC, GTD
MarketToLimitFill what you can as Market; rest unfilled as Limit at last trade priceqty, sideDay, IOC
IcebergDisplay only display_qty on the book; hidden reserve replenishesqty, side, price, display_qtyDay, GTC, GTD

Time-In-Force (TIF) Quick Reference

TIFCodeSurvives EOD?Available for
DaydayNo — cancelled at session closeLimit, Market, Stop, StopLimit, MarketToLimit, Iceberg
GTCgtcYes — persists indefinitelyLimit, Stop, StopLimit, Iceberg
GTDgtdUntil expiry timestampLimit, Stop, StopLimit, Iceberg
IOCiocN/A — cancels immediately after fill attemptLimit, Market, MarketToLimit
FOKfokN/A — fills all or nothingLimit, Market
AtOpenat_openN/A — expires after auction if unfilledLimit
AtCloseat_closeN/A — expires after auction if unfilledLimit

TIF × OrderType Compatibility Matrix

TIF ↓ / Type →LimitMarketStopStopLimitMarketToLimitIceberg
Day✓✓✓✓✓✓
GTC✓✗✓✓✗✓
GTD✓✗✓✓✗✓
IOC✓✓✗✗✓✗
FOK✓✓✗✗✗✗
AtOpen✓✗✗✗✗✗
AtClose✓✗✗✗✗✗

Session States

StateNew Orders?Cancels?Matching?Description
ClosedNoneNoneNoneOutside trading hours
PreOpenLimit, Stop, StopLimit, Iceberg (Day, GTC, GTD, AtOpen)YesNoneOrder collection for opening auction
OpeningAuctionLimit (Day, GTC, GTD, AtOpen)YesNoneUncross at end of window
TradingAll valid type×TIFYesContinuousNormal trading
TradingHaltNoneYesNoneCircuit breaker or manual halt
ReopeningAuctionLimit (Day, GTC, GTD)YesNoneUncross at end; resume trading
ClosingAuctionLimit (Day, GTC, GTD, AtClose)YesNoneUncross; then EOD cleanup

WebSocket Message Types

typePurposeRequired Fields

Server → Client

typeWhen SentKey Fields
order_acceptedEngine confirms orderorder_id, seq, sim_time
order_rejectedPretrade/engine rejectsreason, seq, sim_time
fillPartial or final fillfill_qty, fill_price, is_final, trade_id
order_cancelledCancel processedreason, seq, sim_time
order_expiredOrder expiredseq, sim_time

cancel_rejectedCancel failedreason, seq, sim_time

errorProtocol/business errorcode, message

Reject Reasons

ReasonMeaning
InvalidQuantityQty ≤ 0, below min, above max, or not a multiple of lot_size
InvalidPricePrice ≤ 0, not a multiple of tick_size, or wrong field for order type
PriceBandViolationPrice deviates from reference_price by more than price_band
FatFingerProtectionPrice deviation exceeds risk.fat_finger_pct
SessionNotAcceptingOrdersInstrument is Closed or order type/TIF not allowed in current state
InstrumentHaltedCircuit breaker or manual halt active
UnknownInstrumentInstrument ID not in registry
UnknownParticipantParticipant not in registry
ParticipantNotActiveParticipant has active=false
NotionalLimitExceededqty × price > risk.max_notional_per_order
PositionLimitExceededOrder would exceed participant’s position limit
DuplicateClOrdIdAn active order already exists with this client_order_id
IncompatibleOrderTypeAndTifThe order type and TIF combination is invalid
MarketOrderNoLiquidityMarket order but opposite side has no resting orders
StpCancelledSelf-trade prevention cancelled the order

Cancel Reasons

ReasonMeaning
ClientRequestedCancel sent by the participant
StpCancelAggressiveSTP cancelled the incoming order
StpCancelRestingSTP cancelled the resting order
StpCancelBothSTP cancelled both orders
InstrumentDelistedInstrument removed from trading
SessionEndDay order expired at session close
ReplacedOrder was replaced
ScenarioScenario injection (e.g. expire_all_gtc)

Decimal Encoding (Important!)

All price and quantity values are JSON strings, never numbers.

// Correct
{"price": "38.50", "qty": "100"}

// Wrong — will be rejected
{"price": 38.50, "qty": 100}

This applies to all gateways (WebSocket, HTTP admin API), the event catalog, and the persistence layer. The underlying type is rust_decimal::Decimal.

Config Keys

This document lists every configuration key recognized by NadaTrade’s TOML config file. The authoritative source is crates/nadatrade-bin/src/config.rs.

All keys are optional unless marked required.

[server]

Gateway listen addresses and heartbeat timing.

KeyTypeDefaultDescription
ws_listen_addrString (SocketAddr)"0.0.0.0:9824"IP:port for the WebSocket/HTTP server. Supports IPv4 and IPv6 (bracket form [::1]:9824). The same port serves /v1/orders, /v1/marketdata, /healthz, and /metrics.
fix_listen_addrString (SocketAddr)"0.0.0.0:9823"IP:port for the FIX 4.2 acceptor. Supports IPv4 and IPv6.
fix_heartbeat_secsu6430Seconds between FIX heartbeat messages. Set to 0 to disable outgoing heartbeats. Must be ≥ 0.

Example:

[server]
ws_listen_addr = "127.0.0.1:9824"
fix_listen_addr = "0.0.0.0:9823"
fix_heartbeat_secs = 30

[paths]

Directories for reference data files.

KeyTypeDefaultDescription
instrumentsPathBuf"instruments/"Directory containing .toml instrument definition files. Hot-reloaded via notify.
participantsPathBuf"participants/"Directory containing .toml participant (trader) definition files. Hot-reloaded via notify.

Both paths may be relative (to the working directory) or absolute.

Example:

[paths]
instruments  = "instruments/"
participants = "participants/"

[database]

PostgreSQL connection settings.

KeyTypeDefaultDescription
urlString(none)PostgreSQL connection string. When absent or empty, persistence is disabled (in-memory only). Format: postgres://user:pass@host:port/dbname.
max_connectionsu328Maximum connections in the connection pool. Must be ≥ 1.

Example:

[database]
url = "postgres://nadatrade@localhost:5432/nadatrade"
max_connections = 8

[persist]

Event persistence and snapshot tuning.

KeyTypeDefaultDescription
batch_maxusize500Maximum events to accumulate before flushing to PostgreSQL. Higher values increase throughput but also memory usage and latency.
batch_max_msu6450Maximum milliseconds between flush batches. A flush fires when either batch_max events accumulate or batch_max_ms elapses. Set to 0 to flush every event immediately.
snapshot_every_eventsusize10000Take a full state snapshot every N events. Snapshots accelerate recovery by reducing the number of events that must be replayed.
queue_warn_depthusize5000When the internal event queue reaches this depth, a warning is emitted. Does not block — events continue to accumulate.

Example:

[persist]
batch_max            = 500
batch_max_ms         = 50
snapshot_every_events = 10000
queue_warn_depth     = 100000

[simulation]

SimClock and replay controls.

KeyTypeDefaultDescription
modeString"" (RealTime)Clock driver mode. Valid values: "real_time", "accelerated", "replay". Case-insensitive. An empty string is treated as "real_time".
clock_speedf641.0Time multiplier for accelerated mode. 2.0 = twice as fast, 0.5 = half speed. Ignored in real_time and replay modes. Must be ≥ 0.
sim_startString (ISO 8601)(none)Override the simulation start time. Format: "2026-06-11T00:00:00Z". When omitted, uses the current wall-clock time. Useful for deterministic replay.
replay_feedString (PathBuf)(none)Path to an NDJSON market data replay file. Required when mode = "replay". Each line is a JSON record with market data (trades, quotes).
latency_seedu64(none)Seed for the Box-Muller latency simulator. When omitted, uses system entropy. Set to a fixed value for reproducible latency.

Example:

[simulation]
mode        = "real_time"
clock_speed = 1.0

# Replay mode example:
# mode         = "replay"
# replay_feed  = "data/feed.ndjson"
# sim_start    = "2026-01-02T09:30:00Z"
# latency_seed = 42

Valid modes:

ValueBehavior
"real_time" (or empty)Wall clock drives simulation time 1:1.
"accelerated"Simulation time advances at clock_speed × wall-clock time.
"replay"Simulation time is driven by timestamps from an NDJSON replay feed.

[matching]

Matching engine configuration.

KeyTypeDefaultDescription
book_depth_levelsusize20Maximum price levels visible in the order book snapshot (per side). Deeper levels still exist internally but are not included in market data. Must be ≥ 1.

Example:

[matching]
book_depth_levels = 20

[fix]

FIX 4.2 gateway settings.

KeyTypeDefaultDescription
store_dirString (PathBuf)"data/fix_store"Directory for the per-session FIX message store (append-only journal). Created automatically if it doesn’t exist.
default_roleString"both"Default FIX session role if not specified per-connection. Valid values: "both", "orderentry", "marketdata".
reset_on_logonboolfalseWhen true, resets the message store sequence numbers on every logon. Use only in development.
heartbeat_secsu6430Seconds between FIX heartbeat messages. Must be ≥ 0.
store_capusize1000Maximum messages retained per session in the in-memory store (used for resend requests). Older messages are evicted when the cap is exceeded.

Roles:

ValuePermissions
"both"Session can send orders and receive market data.
"orderentry"Session can only send orders (no market data subscriptions).
"marketdata"Session can only receive market data (no order entry).

Example:

[fix]
store_dir     = "data/fix_store"
default_role  = "both"
reset_on_logon = false
heartbeat_secs = 30
store_cap     = 1000

[tui]

Terminal UI dashboard settings.

KeyTypeDefaultDescription
enabledboolfalseWhen true, launches the ratatui TUI dashboard on startup. The TUI has 5 tabs: Instruments, Order Book, Events, Clock, Ledger.

Example:

[tui]
enabled = true

[logging]

Logging output configuration.

KeyTypeDefaultDescription
levelString"info"Minimum log level. Valid values (case-insensitive): "trace", "debug", "info", "warn", "error".
formatString"pretty"Log output format. Valid values: "pretty" (human-readable, multi-line), "json" (single-line JSON), "compact", "full".

Example:

[logging]
level  = "info"
format = "pretty"

Top-Level Keys

KeyTypeDefaultDescription
ws_jwt_secretString"changeme"HS256 signing secret for WebSocket JWT authentication. Must be changed in production. See below for env: prefix support.

Example:

ws_jwt_secret = "nadatrade-dev-secret-key-change-in-production"

Environment Variable Resolution (env: prefix)

Any config value that accepts a String may use the special env:VARNAME prefix to read the value from an environment variable at config load time. This is resolved after TOML parsing, before the value is passed to the application.

Format:

key = "env:VARIABLE_NAME"

Example:

ws_jwt_secret = "env:NADATRADE_JWT_SECRET"

[database]
url = "env:DATABASE_URL"

When the process loads the config file, a value starting with env: causes the config loader to strip the env: prefix, look up the remaining string as an environment variable name, and replace the value with the variable’s contents.

If the environment variable is not set, the key resolves to an empty string. For keys that are Option<T> (such as database.url), an unset variable resolves to None.

Values that do not start with env: are used as-is with no substitution.

Security considerations:

  • The env: prefix is resolved at startup; changes to environment variables require a restart.
  • Do not commit TOML files containing ws_jwt_secret values to version control. Use env:NADATRADE_JWT_SECRET instead and set the variable in your deployment environment.

FIX Messages

NadaTrade implements the FIX 4.2 protocol over TCP. This reference documents every supported message type, tag table, and wire example. All examples use pipe (|) as the field delimiter for readability; on the wire the delimiter is the ASCII SOH character (\x01).

1. Connection Setup

Transport

Clients connect to NadaTrade via raw TCP. The acceptor binds to a configurable SocketAddr and spawns one tokio task per connection.

BodyLength Framing (tag 9)

FIX messages are delimited by the BodyLength field (tag 9). The value of tag 9 is the number of bytes between the delimiter after tag 9 and the delimiter immediately before tag 10 (the checksum). In other words, it counts the length of the message body starting at the first byte of tag 35.

8=FIX.4.2|9=NNN|35=D|...body...|10=CCC|
                 ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^
                 This is the BodyLength range

Each complete message is extracted from the TCP stream by:

  1. Locating the 8=FIX.4.2| begin string.
  2. Reading the 9= value to determine the body length.
  3. Reading body_length bytes for the body.
  4. Reading the 10=CCC| checksum trailer.

CheckSum (tag 10)

The checksum is the sum of all byte values from (and including) the 8 of 8=FIX.4.2 through the | that immediately precedes tag 10, modulo 256. The result is zero-padded to three digits.

checksum = sum(bytes of "8=FIX.4.2|9=NNN|...body...") % 256

SOH Delimiter

On the wire, fields are separated by ASCII \x01 (Start of Header). In documentation and examples in this reference, | is used instead.

2. Admin Messages

All admin messages include the standard header tags 8, 9, 35, 49, 56, 34 and the trailer tag 10.

Logon (35=A)

Authenticates a session. Must be the first message sent by the client. Until a valid Logon is received, all other messages are rejected with a SessionReject (35=3).

TagNameR/OFormatDescription
49SenderCompIDRStringClient identifier
56TargetCompIDRStringServer identifier (typically NADATRADE)
34MsgSeqNumRu64Sequence number
52SendingTimeOUTCTimestampMessage origination time
98EncryptMethodRintMust be 0 (none)
108HeartBtIntRintHeartbeat interval in seconds
553UsernameRStringParticipant username (custom tag)
554PasswordRStringParticipant password (custom tag)
96RawDataOdataCustom authentication data
141ResetSeqNumFlagOY/NIf Y, reset inbound/outbound sequence numbers

Wire example:

8=FIX.4.2|9=50|35=A|49=BOT001|56=NADATRADE|34=1|553=trader1|554=secret|98=0|108=30|10=CCC|

NadaTrade server response (Logon acceptance):

8=FIX.4.2|9=xx|35=A|49=NADATRADE|56=BOT001|34=1|108=30|98=0|10=CCC|

On failure, a Reject (35=3) is returned with the reason in tag 58.

Logout (35=5)

Requests session termination. Sender may optionally provide a reason.

TagNameR/OFormatDescription
49SenderCompIDRStringClient identifier
56TargetCompIDRStringServer identifier
34MsgSeqNumRu64Sequence number
58TextOStringOptional reason for logout

Wire example:

8=FIX.4.2|9=80|35=5|49=BOT001|56=NADATRADE|34=2|58=End of day|10=CCC|

Heartbeat (35=0)

Sent periodically to keep the session alive. Also used as a response to TestRequest messages.

TagNameR/OFormatDescription
34MsgSeqNumRu64Sequence number
49SenderCompIDRStringSender identifier
56TargetCompIDRStringRecipient identifier
112TestReqIDCStringEchoed from TestRequest; only present when responding to 35=1

Wire example (unsolicited):

8=FIX.4.2|9=xx|35=0|49=NADATRADE|56=BOT001|34=5|10=CCC|

Wire example (reply to TestRequest):

8=FIX.4.2|9=xx|35=0|49=NADATRADE|56=BOT001|34=5|112=test-abc|10=CCC|

TestRequest (35=1)

Sent to verify the connection is alive. The recipient must respond with a Heartbeat echoing the TestReqID.

TagNameR/OFormatDescription
34MsgSeqNumRu64Sequence number
49SenderCompIDRStringSender identifier
56TargetCompIDRStringRecipient identifier
112TestReqIDRStringIdentifier to be echoed in the Heartbeat response

Wire example:

8=FIX.4.2|9=xx|35=1|49=BOT001|56=NADATRADE|34=3|112=alive-check|10=CCC|

ResendRequest (35=2)

Requests retransmission of messages from a range of sequence numbers. NadaTrade replays messages from its persistent MessageStore.

TagNameR/OFormatDescription
34MsgSeqNumRu64Sequence number
49SenderCompIDRStringSender identifier
56TargetCompIDRStringRecipient identifier
7BeginSeqNoRu64First sequence number to replay
16EndSeqNoRu64Last sequence number to replay; 0 means infinity (all from BeginSeqNo onward)

Wire example:

8=FIX.4.2|9=xx|35=2|49=BOT001|56=NADATRADE|34=1|7=5|16=0|10=CCC|

NadaTrade also automatically sends ResendRequests when it detects a sequence gap in inbound messages (see §2.3 below).

Reject (35=3) / SessionReject

Sent when a message cannot be processed. NadaTrade sends this for: unknown message types, role violations, malformed messages, and pre-logon messages.

TagNameR/OFormatDescription
34MsgSeqNumRu64Sequence number
49SenderCompIDRStringSender identifier
56TargetCompIDRStringRecipient identifier
45RefSeqNumOu64Sequence number of rejected message
58TextOStringHuman-readable reason
371RefTagIDOintTag that caused the rejection
372RefMsgTypeOStringMsgType of the rejected message
373SessionRejectReasonOintError code (see §7)

Wire example (SessionReject for unknown message type):

8=FIX.4.2|9=xx|35=3|49=NADATRADE|56=BOT001|34=2|58=Unknown message type: Z|371=35|372=Z|10=CCC|

Wire example (simple Reject):

8=FIX.4.2|9=xx|35=3|49=NADATRADE|56=BOT001|34=2|45=1|58=Invalid credentials|10=CCC|

SequenceReset (35=4)

Resets the sequence number. Used to recover from gaps.

TagNameR/OFormatDescription
34MsgSeqNumRu64Sequence number
49SenderCompIDRStringSender identifier
56TargetCompIDRStringRecipient identifier
36NewSeqNoRu64New sequence number
123GapFillFlagRY/NY = gap fill (messages were administrative), N = hard reset

Wire example:

8=FIX.4.2|9=xx|35=4|49=NADATRADE|56=BOT001|34=10|36=1|123=Y|10=CCC|

Sequence Number Management

NadaTrade maintains independent inbound and outbound sequence numbers per session.

  • Inbound: tracked as last_seq_num_in on the session. When a message arrives with 34 > last_seq_num_in, a ResendRequest (35=2) is sent for the missing range (last_seq_num_in, 0).
  • Outbound: tracked with an AtomicU64 counter. Each outbound message increments it with fetch_add(1, SeqCst).
  • Initiating seq numbers from the client’s Logon: if the Logon contains 141=Y (ResetSeqNumFlag), both sides reset to 1.

3. Order Entry Messages

NewOrderSingle (35=D)

Submits a new order.

TagNameR/OFormatDescription
11ClOrdIDRStringClient-assigned order ID (must be unique)
49SenderCompIDRStringClient identifier
55SymbolRStringInstrument identifier (e.g. PETR4, VALE3)
54SideRchar1 = Buy, 2 = Sell
38OrderQtyRdecimalTotal order quantity
40OrdTypeRchar1 = Market, 2 = Limit, 3 = Stop, 4 = StopLimit, K = MarketToLimit
44PriceCdecimalLimit price; required for Limit and StopLimit; optional for Stop
99StopPxOdecimalStop/trigger price; required for Stop and StopLimit
59TimeInForceOchar0 = Day, 1 = GTC, 2 = AtOpen, 3 = IOC, 4 = FOK, 6 = GTD, 7 = AtClose. Default: 0 (Day)
7928StpModeOcharNadaTrade custom tag. Self-trade prevention mode: 0 = CancelAggressive (default), 1 = CancelResting, 2 = CancelBoth, 3 = None
210MaxFloorOdecimalNadaTrade custom use. Display quantity for iceberg orders (visible portion)
126ExpireTimeCUTCTimestampExpiry time for GTD orders. Format: YYYYMMDD-HH:MM:SS. Required when 59=6

Wire example (Limit order):

8=FIX.4.2|9=123|35=D|49=BOT001|56=NADATRADE|34=2|11=ord-001|55=PETR4|54=1|38=1000|40=2|44=38.50|59=0|10=CCC|

Wire example (Stop order with GTC):

8=FIX.4.2|9=140|35=D|49=BOT001|56=NADATRADE|34=3|11=ord-stop|55=PETR4|54=2|38=1000|40=3|99=37.00|59=1|10=CCC|

Wire example (StopLimit order):

8=FIX.4.2|9=150|35=D|49=BOT001|56=NADATRADE|34=4|11=ord-sl|55=PETR4|54=1|38=100|40=4|44=42.00|99=40.00|59=0|10=CCC|

Wire example (Market order):

8=FIX.4.2|9=123|35=D|49=BOT001|56=NADATRADE|34=5|11=ord-mkt|55=PETR4|54=1|38=500|40=1|59=0|10=CCC|

Wire example (GTD order with expiry):

8=FIX.4.2|9=160|35=D|49=BOT001|56=NADATRADE|34=6|11=ord-gtd|55=PETR4|54=1|38=100|40=2|44=38.50|59=6|126=20261231-23:59:59|10=CCC|

Wire example (Iceberg order with STP):

8=FIX.4.2|9=140|35=D|49=BOT001|56=NADATRADE|34=7|11=ord-ice|55=PETR4|54=1|38=1000|40=2|44=38.50|59=0|210=100|7928=1|10=CCC|

OrderCancelRequest (35=F)

Cancels an existing order.

TagNameR/OFormatDescription
11ClOrdIDRStringUnique ID for this cancel request
41OrigClOrdIDRStringClient order ID of the order to cancel
49SenderCompIDRStringClient identifier
55SymbolRStringInstrument identifier
54SideRchar1 = Buy, 2 = Sell
37OrderIDOStringExchange-assigned order ID (optional)

Wire example:

8=FIX.4.2|9=100|35=F|49=BOT001|56=NADATRADE|34=8|41=ord-001|55=PETR4|54=1|11=cxl-001|10=CCC|

CancelReplaceRequest (35=G)

Amends an existing order’s quantity and/or price. Decreasing quantity preserves time priority; increasing quantity or price change re-enters the order at the back of the queue.

TagNameR/OFormatDescription
11ClOrdIDOStringNew client order ID (defaults to OrigClOrdID if omitted)
41OrigClOrdIDRStringClient order ID of the order to replace
49SenderCompIDRStringClient identifier
55SymbolRStringInstrument identifier
54SideRchar1 = Buy, 2 = Sell
38OrderQtyOdecimalNew total quantity
44PriceOdecimalNew limit price
99StopPxOdecimalNew stop price
7928StpModeOcharNadaTrade custom. New STP mode

Wire example:

8=FIX.4.2|9=150|35=G|49=BOT001|56=NADATRADE|34=9|41=ord-001|55=PETR4|54=1|38=500|44=39.00|11=ord-002|10=CCC|

OrderStatusRequest (35=H) — Planned

Not yet implemented. Planned for a future work package (WP3).

TagNameR/OFormatDescription
11ClOrdIDOStringClient order ID
37OrderIDOStringExchange-assigned order ID

4. Execution Messages

ExecutionReport (35=8)

Sent by the server in response to order lifecycle events: accepted, partially filled, filled, cancelled, replaced, rejected, expired. NadaTrade generates these automatically from ExchangeEvent variants.

TagNameR/OFormatDescription
11ClOrdIDRStringClient order ID
37OrderIDRStringExchange-assigned UUID
17ExecIDOStringUnique execution identifier
150ExecTypeRchar0 = New (accepted), 3 = Done/New, 4 = Cancelled, 5 = Replaced, 8 = Rejected, C = Expired, 1 = Partial Fill, 2 = Fill
39OrdStatusRchar0 = New, 1 = Partially Filled, 2 = Filled, 4 = Cancelled, 8 = Rejected, C = Expired

54SideRchar1 = Buy, 2 = Sell

Wire example (Order Accepted):

Sent when a cancel or cancel/replace request cannot be honoured. NadaTrade generates these from ExchangeEvent::CancelRejected.

TagNameR/OFormatDescription

5. Market Data Messages

MarketDataRequest (35=V)

Subscribes to or unsubscribes from market data for an instrument. NadaTrade enforces session roles: a session configured as OrderEntry-only cannot send this message (it will receive a SessionReject).

TagNameR/OFormatDescription
262MDReqIDRStringUnique request identifier
263SubscriptionRequestTypeRchar0 = Snapshot only, 1 = Snapshot + Updates, 2 = Unsubscribe
264MarketDepthOintMaximum book depth (not currently enforced)
267NoMDEntryTypesRintNumber of entry types in repeating group
269MDEntryTypeRcharRepeating: 0 = Bid, 1 = Ask, 2 = Trade
146NoRelatedSymRintNumber of symbols (always 1 in NadaTrade)
55SymbolRStringInstrument identifier (in repeating group)

Wire example:

8=FIX.4.2|9=100|35=V|49=BOT001|56=NADATRADE|34=11|262=MDREQ1|55=PETR4|263=1|10=CCC|

MarketDataSnapshotFullRefresh (35=W)

Full order book snapshot, sent when a client subscribes or when the book changes for a subscribed instrument. Uses repeating groups for bid/ask entries.

TagNameR/OFormatDescription
262MDReqIDRStringEcho of MDReqID from the subscription
55SymbolRStringInstrument identifier
268NoMDEntriesRintTotal number of price-level entries
269MDEntryTypeRcharRepeating: 0 = Bid, 1 = Ask
270MDEntryPxRdecimalPrice for this level
271MDEntrySizeRdecimalTotal quantity at this level

Wire example (book with 1 bid + 1 ask):

8=FIX.4.2|9=xxx|35=W|49=NADATRADE|56=BOT001|34=12|262=MDREQ1|55=PETR4|269=0|270=38.50|271=1000|269=1|270=38.51|271=500|268=2|10=CCC|

Bids are listed first (descending price), then asks (ascending).

MarketDataIncrementalRefresh (35=X)

Sent when a trade occurs on a subscribed instrument.

TagNameR/OFormatDescription
262MDReqIDRStringEcho of MDReqID from the subscription
55SymbolRStringInstrument identifier
268NoMDEntriesRintNumber of entries (typically 1)
269MDEntryTypeRchar2 = Trade
270MDEntryPxRdecimalTrade price
271MDEntrySizeRdecimalTrade quantity
279MDUpdateActionOchar0 = New, 1 = Change, 2 = Delete

Wire example:

8=FIX.4.2|9=xxx|35=X|49=NADATRADE|56=BOT001|34=13|262=MDREQ1|55=PETR4|268=1|269=2|270=38.50|271=100|10=CCC|

Subscription Tracking

The acceptor maintains a HashMap<String, MdSubscription> per connection. Subscriptions are identified by instrument. On SubscriptionRequestType=2 (unsubscribe), the entry is removed. Market data events (BookUpdated, TradePrinted) are dispatched only to connections subscribing to the relevant instrument.
